Counting surfaces on Calabi-Yau 4-folds II: DT \mathrm{DT} – PT 0 \mathrm{PT}_{0} correspondence
Y. Bae, M. Kool, and H. Park

Abstract
. This is the second part in a series of papers on counting surfaces on Calabi-Yau 4-folds. In this paper, we introduce K K -theoretic DT , PT 0 , PT 1 \mathrm{DT},\mathrm{PT}_{0},\mathrm{PT}_{1} invariants and conjecture a DT \mathrm{DT} – PT 0 \mathrm{PT}_{0} correspondence. For certain tautological insertions, we derive Lefschetz principles in both the compact and toric case allowing reductions to 3-dimensional DT , PT \mathrm{DT},\mathrm{PT} invariants. We also develop a topological vertex and conjecture a DT \mathrm{DT} – PT 0 \mathrm{PT}_{0} vertex correspondence. These methods enable us to verify our conjectures in several examples.



1.
Introduction

1.1.
Overview

This is the second part of a series of papers on counting surfaces on Calabi-Yau 4-folds
[
BKP
]
.
Let
X
be a smooth quasi-projective Calabi-Yau 4-fold, and let

v=(0,0,\gamma,\beta,n-\gamma\cdot\mathrm{td}_{2}(X))\in H^{*}_{c}(X,\mathbb{Q})

be a fixed class in cohomology with compact support. We denote by
\curly I_{v}(X)
the Hilbert scheme of compactly supported closed subschemes
Z\subset X
of dimension
\leq 2
satisfying
\operatorname{ch}(\mathcal{O}_{Z})=v
(and hence
\chi(\mathcal{O}_{Z})=n
).
By
[
OT
]
, we have a virtual cycle in the Chow group with
\mathbb{Q}
-coefficients

(1)

[\curly I_{v}(X)]^{\mathrm{vir}}\in A_{\mathrm{vd}}(\curly I_{v}(X))_{\mathbb{Q}}\,,\quad\mathrm{vd}:=n-\frac{1}{2}\gamma^{2}\,

depending on a choice of orientation on
\curly I_{v}(X)
.
Integration against
[\curly I_{v}(X)]^{\mathrm{vir}}
defines
Donaldson-Thomas
(DT)
invariants
of
X
.

\mathrm{DT}
invariants contain contributions from free moving points on
X
. In order to get better behaved invariants, one should normalize the generating series of
\mathrm{DT}
invariants by the degree zero contribution, i.e., the contribution of Hilbert schemes of points on
X

[
MNOP
,
PT1
,
CKM1
]
.
The aim of this project is to give a moduli-theoretic approach to the normalized
\mathrm{DT}
generating series in the case of surfaces on Calabi-Yau 4-folds.

Question 1.1
.

Is there a moduli-theoretic interpretation of the normalized generating series of
\mathrm{DT}
invariants for surfaces on Calabi-Yau 4-folds?

The analogous question has been answered for curve counting on 3-folds via
stable
(PT)
pairs

[
PT1
]
. A direct extension of their idea leads to a moduli space of pairs
(F,s)
on
X
, which we call
\mathrm{PT}_{1}

pairs
, where
F
is a pure 2-dimensional coherent sheaf on
X
with proper support and
s\in H^{0}(F)
is a section with at most 1-dimensional cokernel. The
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
pairs were independently studied in
[
GJL
]
. However,
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
pairs cannot be used to answer Question
1.1
, because
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
pairs and ideal sheaves differ by both point and curve contributions.

In
[
BKP
]
we introduce a new type of stability condition which sits between the
\mathrm{DT}
and
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
stability.

Definition 1.2
.

Let
F
be a 2-dimensional coherent sheaf with proper support on
X
and
s\in H^{0}(F)
. The pair
(F,s)
is called
\mathrm{PT}_{0}

stable
if
F
has no non-trivial 0-dimensional subsheaf and the cokernel of
s
is
0
-dimensional.

\mathrm{PT}_{0}
stability is not covered by Le Potier’s theory of coherent systems
[
Pot1
]
since the sheaf
F
is generally not pure. In
[
BKP
]
we prove that the moduli space
\curly P^{(0)}_{v}(X)
of
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
pairs is fine, quasi-projective, and it is related to the Hilbert scheme
\curly I_{v}(X)
by GIT wall-crossing.

We use a specific
K
-theoretic insertion for the
\mathrm{DT}
generating series, which we now discuss (for details, see Section
3.2
). By Oh-Thomas
[
OT
]
, there exists a twisted virtual structure sheaf in the Grothendieck group of coherent sheaves

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\curly I}\in K_{0}(\curly I_{v}(X))_{\mathbb{Q}}

which is related to (
1
) via the Riemann-Roch isomorphism.
Let
\mathbb{F}
be the structure sheaf of the universal subscheme in
X\times\curly I_{v}(X)
and
\pi_{\curly I}
the projection to
\curly I_{v}(X)
.
For any line bundle
L
on
X
and a formal variable
y
, we work with the
Nekrasov genus

[
Nek
,
NO
]
(also considered for curves in
[
CKM1
]
)

(2)

\langle\!\langle L\rangle\!\rangle^{\mathrm{DT}}_{X,\gamma,\beta,n}:=\chi(\curly I_{v}(X),\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\curly I}\otimes\widehat{\Lambda}^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}(R\pi_{\curly I*}(\mathbb{F}\otimes L)\otimes y^{-1}))\,,

where we suppress pull-back of
L
from
X
to
X\times\curly I_{v}(X)
. The rank of
R\pi_{\curly I*}(\mathbb{F}\otimes L)
equals
\operatorname{rk}:=(\tfrac{1}{2}L\gamma+\beta)L+n
. We define the analogous invariant for
\curly P_{v}^{(0)}(X)
, in which case we replace the superscript
\mathrm{DT}
by
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
. In some parts, we consider the Nekrasov genera for
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
pairs, in which case we use the superscript
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
.

We propose a
\mathrm{DT}
–
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
correspondence for surface counting:

Conjecture 1.3
.

Let
X
be a projective Calabi-Yau 4-fold and
L
a line bundle on
X
. For any
\gamma\in H^{4}(X,\mathbb{Q})
,
\beta\in H^{6}(X,\mathbb{Q})
, there exist orientations such that

(3)

\frac{\sum_{n}\langle\!\langle L\rangle\!\rangle_{X,\gamma,\beta,n}^{\mathrm{DT}}q^{n}}{\sum_{n}\langle\!\langle L\rangle\!\rangle_{X,0,0,n}^{\mathrm{DT}}q^{n}}=\sum_{n}\langle\!\langle L\rangle\!\rangle_{X,\gamma,\beta,n}^{\mathrm{PT}_{0}}q^{n}.

A formula for the denominator can be found in
[
CK1
,
Par
,
Boj2
]
.
We also provide a quasi-projective and equivariant analog of this conjecture for toric Calabi-Yau 4-folds.
The curve counting
\mathrm{DT}
–
\mathrm{PT}
correspondence for Calabi-Yau 4-folds is studied in
[
CK2
,
CKM1
,
Liu
]
.
Unlike the curve case, the rank
\operatorname{rk}
of the tautological complex generally does not match the degree
\mathrm{vd}
of the virtual class. Remarkably, we claim that the insertion (
2
) remains the
correct
one.
We prove Conjecture
1.3
for various Calabi-Yau 4-fold examples.

(1)

For toric Calabi-Yau
4
-folds, we develop a vertex formalism and propose a vertex
\mathrm{DT}
–
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
correspondence, which we can check in examples (up to certain orders in
q
) using virtual localization.

(2)

For compact Calabi-Yau
4
-folds and
L=\mathcal{O}_{X}(Y)
, for a smooth connected effective divisor
Y
and
\gamma
a class on
Y
, we relate the
\mathrm{DT}
–
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
-correspondence on
X
to the (curve)
\mathrm{DT}
–
\mathrm{PT}
correspondence on
Y
.

1.2.
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
pairs on Calabi-Yau 4-folds

A geometric understanding of
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
pairs serves as a first step towards establishing the
\mathrm{DT}
–
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
correspondence. Let
(F,s)
be a
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
pair with proper scheme theoretic support
Z
on a quasi-projective Calabi-Yau 4-fold
X
. We give a description of
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
pairs with increasing level of complexity:

(1)

If
\operatorname{coker}(s)
is trivial, then
F\cong\mathcal{O}_{Z}
where
Z
has no 0-dimensional connected components or 0-dimensional embedded subschemes. In particular, there are no free moving points.

(2)

If the maximal pure 2-dimensional subscheme
Z_{1}\subset Z
is Cohen-Macaulay, then
\operatorname{coker}(s)
is supported on the
1
-dimensional subscheme
Z\setminus Z_{1}
.

(3)

If
Z
is pure 2-dimensional with non-Cohen-Macaulay singularities, then
\operatorname{coker}(s)
is supported on these non-Cohen-Macaulay singularities.

These cases have more explicit descriptions on toric Calabi-Yau 4-folds as we discuss below (Theorem
1.4
).
Let
X
be a smooth quasi-projective toric Calabi-Yau 4-fold. Let
T_{X}\cong(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{4}
be the 4-dimensional torus acting on
X
and let
T:=\{t_{1}t_{2}t_{3}t_{4}=1\}\leq T_{X}
be the
Calabi-Yau torus
preserving the holomorphic volume form.
The action of
T_{X}
lifts to the moduli space
\curly P^{(0)}_{v}(X)
.

To simplify the discussion, let us consider a single toric chart
U_{\alpha}\cong\mathbb{C}^{4}
of
X
(details can be found in Section
5.2
). The
(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{4}
-fixed
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
pairs on
\mathbb{C}^{4}
with scheme theoretic support
Z\subset\mathbb{C}^{4}
correspond to the finitely generated
(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{4}
-invariant submodules of

(4)

\varinjlim\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}(\mathfrak{m}^{r},\mathcal{O}_{Z})/\mathcal{O}_{Z},

where
\mathfrak{m}\subset\mathcal{O}_{Z}
denotes the ideal sheaf of the origin.
The pure part
Z_{1}\subset Z
of the support is described by six finite partitions (i.e., piles of boxes in
\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{2}
)
\boldsymbol{\lambda}=\{\lambda_{ab}\}_{1\leq a<b\leq 4}
.
Consider the subscheme

W=(Z_{\lambda_{12}}\cup Z_{\lambda_{13}}\cup Z_{\lambda_{23}})\cap(Z_{\lambda_{14}}\cup Z_{\lambda_{24}}\cup Z_{\lambda_{34}}),

where
Z_{\lambda_{ab}}
is the pure 2-dimensional subscheme set-theoretically supported on
\mathbb{C}^{2}\cong Z(x_{a},x_{b})\subset\mathbb{C}^{4}
with scheme structure determined by
\lambda_{ab}
(Section
5.2
). Then
Z_{1}
is Cohen-Macaulay if and only if
T_{0}(\mathcal{O}_{W})\subset\mathcal{O}_{W}
is trivial.
1
1

1

For any coherent sheaf
E
, we denote by
T_{i}(E)\subset E
its maximal
i
-dimensional subsheaf.

We give an explicit description of (
4
) which determines
(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{4}
-fixed
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
pairs. An analogous description of
(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{4}
-fixed
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
pairs is given in Section
5.2
.

Theorem 1.4
.

Let
(F,s)
be a
(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{4}
-fixed
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
pair on
\mathbb{C}^{4}
with scheme theoretic support
Z
. Let
Z_{1}\subset\mathbb{C}^{4}
be the pure part of
Z
.

\mathrm{(1)}

The induced sequence

0\to\varinjlim\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}(\mathfrak{m}^{r},T_{1}(\mathcal{O}_{Z}))\to\varinjlim\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}(\mathfrak{m}^{r},\mathcal{O}_{Z})\to\varinjlim\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}(\mathfrak{m}^{r},\mathcal{O}_{Z_{1}})\to 0

is exact and there is a
(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{4}
-equivariant isomorphism

\varinjlim\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}(\mathfrak{m}^{r},\mathcal{O}_{Z_{1}})/\mathcal{O}_{Z_{1}}\cong T_{0}(\mathcal{O}_{W}).

\mathrm{(2)}

Suppose
Z=Z_{1}
. Then the
(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{4}
-fixed
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
pairs on
\mathbb{C}^{4}
with scheme theoretic support
Z
are in bijective correspondence with the (finitely many) closed points of

\bigcup_{m\geq 0}{\curly Quot}_{W}(T_{0}(\mathcal{O}_{W}),m)^{(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{4}}\,,

where
{\curly Quot}_{W}(T_{0}(\mathcal{O}_{W}),m)
denotes the Quot scheme of length
m
0-dimensional quotients of
T_{0}(\mathcal{O}_{W})
.

Example 1.5
.

Consider
Z=S_{1}\cup S_{2}\subset\mathbb{C}^{4}
, where
S_{1}=Z(x_{1},x_{2})
and
S_{2}=Z(x_{3},x_{4})
. This is the simplest example of a non-Cohen-Macaulay singularity.
The
(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{4}
-fixed
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
pairs on
\mathbb{C}^{4}
with scheme theoretic support
Z
correspond to elements of the Hilbert schemes of points
\operatorname{Hilb}^{m}(S_{1}\cap S_{2})
.
Therefore, we have
m=0,1
and there are only two fixed points corresponding to the trivial pair
\mathcal{O}_{Z}\to 0
and the extension
0\to\mathcal{O}_{Z}\to\mathcal{O}_{S_{1}}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{S_{2}}\to\mathcal{O}_{S_{1}\cap S_{2}}\to 0
.

1.3.
Vertex formalism

For toric Calabi-Yau 4-folds
X
, we use the virtual localization formula
[
OT
]
to compute equivariant
\mathrm{DT},\mathrm{PT}_{0}
, and
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
invariants.

Denote by
V(X)
the collection of
T_{X}
-fixed points of
X
, by
E(X)
the collection of
T_{X}
-fixed projective lines in
X
, and by
F(X)
the collection of
T_{X}
-fixed irreducible smooth projective toric surfaces in
X
. We refer to elements of
V(X)
,
E(X)
,
F(X)
as vertices, edges, and faces. To a vertex
\alpha\in V(X)
corresponds a
T_{X}
-fixed point
p_{\alpha}
and to an edge
\alpha\beta\in E(X)
corresponds a
T_{X}
-fixed
\mathbb{P}^{1}\subset X
passing through
p_{\alpha},p_{\beta}
. To a face
f\in F(X)
corresponds a
T_{X}
-fixed surface
S_{f}\subset X
.
Each
p_{\alpha}
lies in a toric chart
U_{\alpha}\cong\mathbb{C}^{4}
, which jointly cover
X
.

Let
\curly I:=\curly I_{v}(X)
.
Then
\curly I^{T_{X}}=\curly I^{T}
and it is 0-dimensional and reduced. On each chart
U_{\alpha}
, a 2-dimensional
T_{X}
-fixed subscheme
Z_{\alpha}\subset U_{\alpha}
corresponds to an infinite solid partition
\pi^{(\alpha)}
. The solid partition
\pi^{(\alpha)}
can be visualized as a pile of boxes in
\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{4}
with a limiting plane partition (i.e., pile of boxes in
\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{3}
as in
[
MNOP
]
) along each of the four coordinate directions
x_{i}\gg 0
. Therefore,
T_{X}
-fixed 2-dimensional subschemes
Z\in\curly I^{T_{X}}
correspond to certain collections of solid partitions
\boldsymbol{\pi}=\{\pi^{(\alpha)}\}_{\alpha\in V(X)}
satisfying gluing conditions described in Sections
5.3
. Consider the
K
-theory class of the virtual tangent bundle

(5)

T^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\curly I}|_{Z}=R\operatorname{Hom}_{X}(I_{Z/X},I_{Z/X})_{0}[1]\in K^{0}_{T_{X}}(\mathrm{pt})\cong\mathbb{Z}[t_{1}^{\pm 1},t_{2}^{\pm 1},t_{3}^{\pm 1},t_{4}^{\pm 1}],

where
I_{Z/X}\subset\mathcal{O}_{X}
denotes the ideal sheaf of
Z
and
(\cdot)_{0}
the trace-free part.

After redistribution, the
K
-theory class (
5
) can be separated into vertex
\mathsf{V}_{\alpha}
, edge
\mathsf{E}_{\alpha\beta}
, and face
\mathsf{F}_{f}
contributions, defined in Section
6.1
in terms of
\boldsymbol{\pi}
, which are Laurent
polynomials
in
t_{1},t_{2},t_{3},t_{4}
.

Theorem 1.6
.

Let
X
be a toric Calabi-Yau 4-fold and
Z\subset X
a 2-dimensional
T_{X}
-fixed closed subscheme with proper support. Then we have

T^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\curly I}|_{Z}=R\operatorname{Hom}_{X}(I_{Z/X},I_{Z/X})_{0}[1]=\sum_{\alpha\in V(X)}\mathsf{V}_{\alpha}+\sum_{\alpha\beta\in E(X)}\mathsf{E}_{\alpha\beta}+\sum_{f\in F(X)}\mathsf{F}_{f},

where
\mathsf{V}_{\alpha},\mathsf{E}_{\alpha\beta},\mathsf{F}_{f}
are Laurent polynomials in
t_{1},t_{2},t_{3},t_{4}
.

For curve counting theory on
3
-folds, the redistribution was developed in
[
MNOP
,
PT2
]
. The surface asymptotics adds new features to the redistribution process. We establish the analog of this theorem for any
T_{X}
-fixed
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
and
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
pair on
X
with proper support in Section
6.1
.

Let
\curly P:=\curly P_{v}^{(0)}(X)
. Unlike the
\mathrm{DT}
case, in general we no longer have “
\curly P^{T_{X}}=\curly P^{T}
, and it is 0-dimensional and reduced” (and similarly in the
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
case). Henceforth, let us assume we are in a case where
\curly P^{T_{X}}=\curly P^{T}
, and it is 0-dimensional and reduced. The same issue appears for
\mathrm{PT}
curve theory on toric Calabi-Yau 4-folds. Then this assumption is rather restrictive and essentially rules out 3 or 4 legs coming together in any chart
U_{\alpha}

[
PT2
,
CKM1
]
. In fact, the union of all
(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{4}
-fixed loci in the 4-leg case satisfies Murphy’s Law
[
Sch
]
.
However, by the following proposition, for
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
theory, our assumption is satisfied in many interesting “truly 4-dimensional” examples.

Theorem 1.7
.

Let
\curly P:=\curly P^{(0)}_{v}(X)
. For all
(F,s)\in\curly P^{T_{X}}
, suppose either (1) the sheaf
F
is pure, or (2) the scheme theoretic support of
F|_{U_{\alpha}}
on each chart
U_{\alpha}
has at most two embedded 1-dimensional components and its pure part is Cohen-Macaulay.
Then
\curly P^{T_{X}}=\curly P^{T}
and it is 0-dimensional and reduced.

The combinatorial description of edge (resp. face) terms associated to
\mathrm{DT}
/
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
pairs is related to vertex (resp. edge) terms of
\mathrm{DT}
/
\mathrm{PT}
pairs on toric 3-folds.
Fix plane partitions
\boldsymbol{\mu}=\{\mu_{a}\}_{a=1}^{4}
corresponding to
(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{3}
-fixed subschemes of
\mathbb{C}^{3}
of dimension
\leq 1
, which are the “asymptotic cross-sections” that we fix along the
x_{1},x_{2},x_{3},x_{4}
-axes. We twist each vertex contribution of (
39
) by the Nekrasov insertion (
2
) and define the
K
-theoretic
\mathrm{DT}
and
\mathrm{PT}_{0}

topological vertex

(6)

\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{DT}}_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}\mu_{4}}(q),\quad\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{PT}_{0}}_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}\mu_{4}}(q)\,,

where (
6
) depend on a choice of
\pm\sqrt{\cdot}
assigned to each fixed point.

We conjecture the following
\mathrm{DT}
–
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
topological vertex correspondence.

Conjecture 1.8
.

For plane partitions
\boldsymbol{\mu}=\{\mu_{a}\}_{a=1}^{4}
as above, there exists a choice of
\pm\sqrt{\cdot}
assigned to the
(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{4}
-fixed points such that

\frac{\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{DT}}_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}\mu_{4}}(q)}{\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{DT}}_{\varnothing\varnothing\varnothing\varnothing}(q)}=\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{PT}_{0}}_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}\mu_{4}}(q).

The denominator
is given by the Magnificent Four formula
[
Nek
,
KR
]
. For curves counting on toric
3
-folds, the
\mathrm{DT}/\mathrm{PT}
vertex and vertex correspondence has been studied in
[
AKMV
,
MNOP
,
MNOP2
,
PT2
,
NO
]
. Its numerical version was proved combinatorially in
[
JWY
]
and its full
K
-theoretic version was recently established in
[
KLT
]
. For curve counting theories on toric Calabi-Yau 4-folds, the
\mathrm{DT}/\mathrm{PT}
vertex correspondence has been studied in
[
CKM1
,
Liu
]
.

We check Conjecture
1.8
, up to certain order in
q
, by direct calculation in several examples (Proposition
6.19
).

Example 1.9
.

Suppose we fix
\boldsymbol{\mu}
such that the minimal
(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{4}
-fixed 2-dimensional closed subscheme
Z\subset\mathbb{C}^{4}
with asymptotics
\boldsymbol{\mu}
is
pure
. Then, by Theorem
1.4
(ii),
\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{PT}_{0}}_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}\mu_{4}}(q)
is a Laurent
polynomial
in
q
. Moreover, its number of terms is at most one plus the length of the 0-dimensional sheaf
\curly Ext^{3}(\mathcal{O}_{Z},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}^{4}})
, which measures how far
Z
is from being Cohen-Macaulay (Proposition
2.4
). For
Z=S_{1}\cup S_{2}
as in Example
1.5
,
\curly Ext^{3}(\mathcal{O}_{Z},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}^{4}})
has length 1 and

\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{PT}_{0}}_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}\mu_{4}}(q)=\frac{[t_{1}t_{3}][t_{2}t_{3}]}{[y]}q^{-1}+[t_{1}t_{2}],

where
[x]:=x^{\frac{1}{2}}-x^{-\frac{1}{2}}
. In Figure
1
(left), we depict the
Z(x_{4})
-slice of the solid partition corresponding to
S_{1}\cup S_{2}
. The blue boxes have no boxes stacked on top of them in the positive
x_{4}
-direction, whereas the purple boxes have infinitely many boxes stacked on top in the positive
x_{4}
-direction. The box at
(0,0,0,0)
is purple. On the
\mathrm{DT}
-side, the
(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{4}
-fixed points are obtained by stacking boxes “on top” of this configuration as in Figure
1
(right). On the
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
-side, there are only two
(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{4}
-fixed points corresponding to putting zero or one box at the intersection
S_{1}\cap S_{2}
(Example
1.5
).

Adding boxes to
S_{1}\cup S_{2}
.

We show that Conjecture
1.8
implies the
global

T
-equivariant
K
-theoretic
\mathrm{DT}
–
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
correspondence.

Proposition 1.10
.

Let
L
be a
T_{X}
-equivariant line bundle on
X
. Let
\gamma\in H_{c}^{4}(X,\mathbb{Q})
and
\beta\in H_{c}^{6}(X,\mathbb{Q})
such that
\curly P_{v}^{(0)}(X)^{T}=\curly P_{v}^{(0)}(X)^{T_{X}}
is empty or 0-dimensional and reduced for
v=(0,0,\gamma,\beta,n-\gamma\cdot\mathrm{td}_{2}(X))
and all
n
. Suppose Conjecture
1.8
holds. Then there are choices of square roots such that the equivariant form of (
3
) holds.

Our vertex formalism is useful for calculating
\mathrm{DT},\mathrm{PT}_{0},\mathrm{PT}_{1}
invariants for
global
toric geometries. There is a huge advantage of
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
and
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
theory over
\mathrm{DT}
theory — since there are no free moving points the moduli space can be compact (Proposition
3.12
).

Example 1.11
.

For
X=\mathrm{Tot}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(-2)\oplus\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(-1))
, the
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
and
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
moduli spaces are compact. Using the vertex formalism, we determined the following formulae
2
2

2

The
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
calculations in Examples
1.11
,
1.12
are subject to Conjecture
5.30
: if
\curly P^{T_{X}}
is 0-dimensional (e.g. for local surfaces), then
\curly P^{T_{X}}=\curly P^{T}
and it is 0-dimensional and reduced.
for certain
m,\delta,n

\displaystyle\langle\!\langle\mathcal{O}_{X}\rangle\!\rangle_{1,\frac{5}{2},m}^{\mathrm{PT}_{0}}

\displaystyle=[y](y^{\frac{m-1}{2}}+y^{-\frac{m-1}{2}}),

\displaystyle\langle\!\langle\mathcal{O}_{X}\rangle\!\rangle_{1,\frac{3}{2}+\delta,n}^{\mathrm{PT}_{1}}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{CO}_{\chi(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(\delta))-n}(\mathbb{P}^{2},\mathcal{O}(-1))[y](y^{\frac{n-1}{2}}+\cdots+y^{-\frac{n-1}{2}}),

where
m>3
,
\delta\geq 0
, and
1\leq n\leq\chi(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(\delta))
, and
\mathsf{CO}_{i}(\mathbb{P}^{2},\mathcal{O}(-1))
are the Carlsson-Okounkov numbers associated to the
\mathcal{O}(-1)
-twisted tangent bundles of Hilbert schemes of points on
\mathbb{P}^{2}
(see Section
6.6
).

The formulae in the previous example are for primitive
\gamma
and are in fact proved using non-toric methods for
all

m,\delta,n
in a sequel to this paper. However, the vertex formalism allows us to explore the non-primitive case:

Example 1.12
.

Continuing the notation of the previous example, we define

\mathsf{G}^{\mathrm{PT}_{1}}_{d}(q,Q,y):=\sum_{m,n}\langle\!\langle\mathcal{O}_{X}\rangle\!\rangle_{d,m,n}^{\mathrm{PT}_{1}}Q^{m}q^{n}.

One benefit of the
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
generating series is that, for fixed
d,m
, it has only finitely many terms
[
BKP
, Prop. 2.14]
. Using the vertex formalism, for appropriate square roots we find

\displaystyle[y]^{-16}\mathsf{G}^{\mathrm{PT}_{1}}_{4}=

\displaystyle\,q^{16}Q^{11}+\Big(8q^{16}+4(y^{\frac{1}{2}}+y^{-\frac{1}{2}})q^{17}+4(y+y^{-1})q^{18}+

\displaystyle 4(y^{\frac{3}{2}}+y^{-\frac{3}{2}})q^{19}+10(y^{2}+8y+12+8y^{-1}+y^{-2})q^{20}\Big)Q^{12}+\cdots,

where
\cdots
means higher order terms in
Q
. For more examples, see Section
6.6
.

1.4.
Virtual Lefschetz principle

Let
X
be a projective Calabi-Yau 4-fold and let
\iota:Y\hookrightarrow X
be smooth connected effective divisor inducing an injection
\iota_{*}:H_{*}(Y,\mathbb{Q})\hookrightarrow H_{*}(X,\mathbb{Q})
. Let
L:=\mathcal{O}_{X}(Y)
,
v^{\prime}\in H^{*}(Y,\mathbb{Q})
, and

v:=(0,0,\gamma,\beta,n-\gamma\cdot\mathrm{td}_{2}(X))=\iota_{*}v^{\prime}\cdot\mathrm{td}(L)^{-1}\in H^{*}(X,\mathbb{Q}).

Let
q\in\{-1,0,1\}
and consider the moduli spaces
\curly P_{X}=\curly P^{(q)}_{v}(X)
and
\curly P_{Y}:=\curly P_{v^{\prime}}^{(q)}(Y)
. Then we have a closed embedding
\imath=\iota_{*}:\curly P_{Y}\hookrightarrow\curly P_{X}
.

We relate surface counting on
X
to surface counting on the divisor
Y
.

Theorem 1.13
.

Suppose
H^{>0}(X,F\otimes L)=0
for all
(F,s)\in\curly P_{X}
,
H^{2}(Y,F)=0
for all
(F,s)\in\curly P_{Y}
, and
\imath
induces an injective map on the sets of connected components of
\curly P_{Y}
and
\curly P_{X}
. Then there exists an orientation such that

\imath_{*}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\curly P_{Y}}=\widehat{\Lambda}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}(R\pi_{\curly P_{X}*}(\mathbb{F}\otimes L))^{\vee}\otimes\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\curly P_{X}}.

We use the third-named author’s virtual pullback formula
[
Par
]
for the proof. Next, we identify surface counting invariants on
X
with curve counting invariants on
Y
by “twisting away
\mathcal{O}_{Y}(S)
” when
S\subset Y
is the unique pure 2-dimensional subscheme in class
[S]\in H^{2}(Y,\mathbb{Q})
.

Corollary 1.14
.

Continuing the setting of Theorem
1.13
, suppose
S\subset Y
is the only pure 2-dimensional subscheme in class
[S]\in H^{2}(Y,\mathbb{Q})
and let
\gamma:=\iota_{*}[S]
. Let
v^{\prime\prime}:=1-(1-v^{\prime})e^{[S]}\in H^{*}(Y,\mathbb{Q})
and let
\beta^{\prime\prime}:=v_{2}^{\prime\prime}\in H^{4}(Y,\mathbb{Q})
. Suppose that, for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
and
q\in\{-1,0\}
, the inclusion maps
\curly P^{(q)}_{v^{\prime}}(Y)\hookrightarrow\curly P^{(q)}_{v}(X)
induce injective maps on the sets of connected components. Then, for
y=1
, there exist orientations such that

\displaystyle\sum_{n}\langle\!\langle L\rangle\!\rangle_{X,\gamma,\beta,n}^{\mathrm{DT}}q^{n}=q^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\sum_{n}\langle\!\langle 1\rangle\!\rangle_{Y,\beta^{\prime\prime},n}^{\mathrm{DT}}q^{n},\quad\sum_{n}\langle\!\langle L\rangle\!\rangle_{X,\gamma,\beta,n}^{\mathrm{PT}_{0}}q^{n}=q^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\sum_{n}\langle\!\langle 1\rangle\!\rangle_{Y,\beta^{\prime\prime},n}^{\mathrm{PT}}q^{n},

where
q^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
is and overall factor with power depending only on
S,Y,X,\beta^{\prime\prime}
and the right hand sides are the
K
-theoretic
\mathrm{DT}
/
\mathrm{PT}
generating series of
Y

[
NO
]
.

In the setting of the corollary, we deduce that the
\mathrm{DT}
–
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
correspondence (Conjecture
1.3
) for
X
,
L
,
\gamma
,
\beta
,
y=1
is equivalent to the (curve)
\mathrm{DT}
–
\mathrm{PT}
correspondence for
Y
,
\beta^{\prime\prime}
. In particular, it holds in the following three cases (1) when
\beta^{\prime\prime}=0
, (2) when
Y
is a Calabi-Yau 3-fold by
[
Bri
,
Tod
]
, and (3) when
Y
is a toric 3-fold by
[
KLT
]
. We present two examples.
3
3

3

In the following two examples, we assume that the condition on connected components in the corollary is satisfied.

Example 1.15
.

Let
Y\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
be a general Weierstraß fibration with unique section
\mathbb{P}^{2}\subset Y
. Let
X=Y\times E
, where
E
is a smooth elliptic curve. Let
\gamma=[\mathbb{P}^{2}]
and
\beta=(\epsilon+\tfrac{3}{2})[\mathbb{P}^{1}]
, where
\mathbb{P}^{1}\subset\mathbb{P}^{2}
is the class of a line on the section and
\epsilon=0,1
. Then for
L=\mathcal{O}_{X}(Y\times\{\mathrm{pt}\})
and
y=1
, the
\mathrm{DT}
–
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
correspondence of Conjecture
1.3
holds. For
\epsilon=1
, it reduces to the
\mathrm{DT}
–
\mathrm{PT}
correspondence for curve class
[\mathbb{P}^{1}]
of the Calabi-Yau 3-fold
Y

[
Bri
,
Tod
]
.

Example 1.16
.

Let
Y
be the blow-up of
\mathbb{P}^{3}
in a point and let
\mathbb{P}^{2}\subset Y
be the class of the exceptional divisor. Let
X\to Y
be a general Weierstraß fibration with section, so we have
\mathbb{P}^{2}\subset Y\subset X
. Let
\gamma=[\mathbb{P}^{2}]
and
\beta=(\epsilon-\tfrac{1}{2})[\mathbb{P}^{1}]
, where
\mathbb{P}^{1}\subset\mathbb{P}^{2}
is the class of a line on the exceptional divisor and
\epsilon=0,1
. Then for
L=\mathcal{O}_{X}(Y)
and
y=1
, the
\mathrm{DT}
–
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
correspondence of Conjecture
1.3
holds. For
\epsilon=1
, it reduces to the
K
-theoretic
\mathrm{DT}
–
\mathrm{PT}
correspondence of the Fano 3-fold
Y
for curve class
[\mathbb{P}^{1}]

[
KLT
]
.

We also provide a second virtual Lefschetz principle when
Y
is a smooth quasi-projective toric 3-fold and
X=\mathrm{Tot}(K_{Y})
by combining the virtual localization formula with the spectral construction (Theorem
4.10
).

1.5.
Relations to physics

Our vertex formalism for
q=-1
is related to recent work of Nekrasov-Piazzalunga
[
NP2
]
.
Their work originates from physics, namely maximally supersymmetric
(8+1)
-dimensional gauge theory on a toric Calabi-Yau 4-fold fibred over a circle or, string-theoretically, the counting of BPS states of a system of D0–D2–D4–D6–D8 branes on a toric Calabi-Yau 4-fold in the presence of a large Neveu-Schwarz
B
-field. We provide the mathematical explanation for why the vertex, edge, and face terms in
[
NP2
]
calculate the virtual tangent bundle (
5
).
As opposed to loc. cit., which only concerns the vertex formalism for the
q=-1
case, we consider all three cases
q=-1,0,1
. For
q=0,1
, there are many examples of
compact
moduli spaces, whereas this almost never happens for
q=-1
due to free roaming points. This allows us to provide global computations of
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
invariants (Proposition
3.12
, Section
6.6
).

1.6.
Open questions

We addressed the
\mathrm{DT}
–
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
correspondence among the three stability conditions for pairs. It is interesting to find relations between
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
and
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
invariants. In a sequel, we will study this direction.

In
[
BKP
]
we introduced
reduced
virtual cycles for surface counting on Calabi-Yau 4-folds. We expect Conjecture
1.3
to hold for reduced invariants. See Remark
3.11
for why reducing does not play a significant role in this paper.
Furthermore, it is interesting to find the connection between Conjecture
1.3
and the new wall-crossing formalism introduced in
[
GJT
,
Boj2
]
.

It is desirable to remove the condition “
\curly P^{T_{X}}=\curly P^{T}
, and it is 0-dimensional reduced”. For curve counting on Calabi-Yau 4-folds, this is pursued in
[
Liu
]
.

Our formulae depend on a choice of square root assigned to the fixed points. We expect that there exist
canonical global orientations
on
\curly P
which induce these signs. In all examples we calculated, our formulae hold for very special (often unique) choices of
\pm\sqrt{\cdot}
. For Hilbert schemes of points on
\mathbb{C}^{4}
, sign formulae were conjectured in
[
NP1
]
and proved using global orientations in
[
KR
]
.

1.7.
Notation and conventions

We use the following notation and conventions throughout the paper.

•

All schemes and algebraic stacks are assumed to be of finite type over the complex field
\mathbb{C}
, unless stated otherwise.
A
variety
is a separated integral scheme.
A
point

x\in X
in a scheme or an algebraic stack
X
means a
\mathbb{C}
-valued point.

•

A
Calabi-Yau
n
-fold
is a smooth quasi-projective variety
X
of dimension
n
with
K_{X}\cong\mathcal{O}_{X}
.

•

For a coherent sheaf
F
on a smooth quasi-projective variety
X
, we use the following notation for the various duals:

–

F^{\vee}:=R\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{X}(F,\mathcal{O}_{X})
;

–

F^{D}:=\curly Ext^{c}_{X}(F,K_{X})
, where
c
is the codimension of
F
in
X
.

•

For a smooth quasi-projective variety
X
, we denote by

–

\mathrm{Coh}(X)
the abelian category of coherent sheaves on
X
;

–

\mathrm{Coh}_{\leq q}(X):=\{F\in\mathrm{Coh}(X):\dim(F)\leq q\}
;

–

\mathrm{Coh}_{\geq q+1}(X):=\{F\in\mathrm{Coh}(X):T_{q}(F)=0\}
, where
T_{q}(F)\subset F
is the maximal
q
-dimensional torsion subsheaf of
F
;

–

D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}(X)
the bounded derived category of coherent sheaves on
X
.

We also consider the full subcategories
\mathrm{Coh}(X)_{c}
,
\mathrm{Coh}_{\leq q}(X)_{c}
,
\mathrm{Coh}_{\geq q+1}(X)_{c}
of coherent sheaves with proper (“compact”) support.

•

For a quasi-projective scheme
M
, let
K_{0}(M)
be the Grothendieck group of coherent sheaves on
M
and
K^{0}(M)
the Grothendieck group of locally free sheaves on
M
. For an algebraic torus
T
acting on
M
, we denote the
T
-equivariant versions of these
K
-groups by
K_{0}^{T}(M)
and
K^{0}_{T}(M)
. We also work with
K
-groups of coherent sheaves with proper (“compact”) support, which we denote by
K_{0}(M)_{c}
,
K_{0}^{T}(M)_{c}
.

•

For any
V\in K^{0}(M)
with
\operatorname{rk}(V)=1
, there is a well-defined square-root operation
V^{\frac{1}{2}}\in K^{0}(M,\mathbb{Z}[\tfrac{1}{2}])

[
OT
, Lem. 5.1]
.

•

For any rank
r
vector bundle
V
on
M
, we write
\Lambda_{x}V:=\sum_{i=0}^{r}[\Lambda^{i}V]x^{i}\in K^{0}(M)[x]
. This operation can be extended to
K^{0}(M)[[x]]
by setting

\Lambda_{x}(-V)=1/\Lambda_{x}V=\operatorname{Sym}_{-x}V\in K^{0}(M)[[x]].

Whenever it is well-defined, e.g. in the case
M
has trivial
T
-action and
V\in K^{0}_{T}(M)
has no
T
-fixed part with negative coefficients, we set
\Lambda^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}V:=\Lambda_{-1}V
. Finally, we define a normalized version

\displaystyle\widehat{\Lambda}^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}V

\displaystyle:=(\Lambda^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}V)\otimes\det(V)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\in K^{0}(M,\mathbb{Z}[\tfrac{1}{2}]).

•

For any quasi-projective scheme
M
, we denote by
A_{*}(M)
the Chow group with
\mathbb{Q}
-coefficients. For any
d\in\mathbb{Q}\setminus\mathbb{Z}
, we let
A_{d}(M):=0
.

•

For a morphism
f:M\to N
of quasi-projective schemes, we denote by
\mathbb{L}_{M/N}
the full cotangent complex
[
Ill
]
and by
\tau^{\geq-1}\mathbb{L}_{M/N}
the truncated cotangent complex. In the case
N=\operatorname{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}
, we write
\mathbb{L}_{M}:=\mathbb{L}_{M/\operatorname{Spec}\,\mathbb{C}}
.

•

For any
d
-dimensional smooth toric variety
Y
defined by a fan
\Sigma
in a lattice
N\cong\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, we always assume that all cones of
\Sigma
are contained in a
d
-dimensional cone. This implies that
Y
can be covered by
(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{d}
-invariant copies of
\mathbb{C}^{d}

[
Ful
]
.

2.
Preliminaries

2.1.
Basic properties of stable pairs

Let
X
be a smooth quasi-projective variety of dimension
n
over the complex field
\mathbb{C}
. We first recall the following definition from
[
BKP
]
.

Definition 2.1
.

Let
F
be a coherent sheaf on
X
with 2-dimensional proper support and let
s:\mathcal{O}_{X}\to F
be a section.
For
q\in\{-1,0,1\}
, we say that the pair
(F,s)
is
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
stable
if

F\in\mathrm{Coh}_{\geq q+1}(X)Q:=\operatorname{coker}(\mathcal{O}_{X}\xrightarrow{s}F)\in\mathrm{Coh}_{\leq q}(X).

We also write
\mathrm{DT}:=\mathrm{PT}_{-1}
.

The following results were proved in
[
BKP
]
when
X
is projective. The proofs in loc. cit. also work in the quasi-projective case.

Lemma 2.2
.

Given a
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pair
(F,s)
on
X
, the scheme theoretic support of the coherent sheaf
F
is the scheme theoretic support of the section
s
.

Proposition 2.3
.

Let
W\subseteq Z\subseteq X
be closed subschemes such that

\mathcal{O}_{Z}\in\mathrm{Coh}_{\geq q+1}(X)
and
\mathcal{O}_{W}\in\mathrm{Coh}_{\leq q}(X)
. Suppose
W
is reduced
and denote its ideal by
\mathfrak{I}\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{Z}
.
Then
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pairs
(F,s)
with support
Z
and cokernel
Q
satisfying
\mathrm{Supp}(Q)^{\mathrm{red}}\subseteq W
are equivalent to coherent subsheaves of
\varinjlim\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{X}(\mathfrak{I}^{r},\mathcal{O}_{Z})/\mathcal{O}_{Z}
.

Proposition 2.4
.

Let
S
be a
pure
2-dimensional subscheme of
X
. Then the following three conditions are equivalent:

\mathrm{(i)}

S
is not Cohen-Macaulay.

\mathrm{(ii)}

\curly Ext^{n-1}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},K_{X})\neq 0
.

\mathrm{(iii)}

There exists a
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
pair
(F,s)
with support
S
and non-zero cokernel
Q
.

Furthermore, for any
m\geq 0
, the
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
pairs
(F,s)
with support
S
and
\chi(Q)=m
are in bijective correspondence with the closed points of the Quot scheme

{\curly Quot}_{X}(\curly Ext^{n-1}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},K_{X}),m)

parametrizing length
m
quotients of the 0-dimensional sheaf
\curly Ext^{n-1}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},K_{X})
.

Proposition 2.5
.

For any
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
pair
(F,s)
on
X
such that
S=\mathrm{Supp}(F)^{\mathrm{pure}}
is Cohen-Macaulay, we have a natural quasi-isomorphism

I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\cong[I_{S/X}\to T_{1}(F)],

where
T_{1}(F)
is zero or pure 1-dimensional and
I_{S/X}
denotes the ideal sheaf of
S
in
X
.

In particular, when
n=3
, we have
I_{S/X}=\mathcal{O}_{X}(-S)
and

I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\cong[\mathcal{O}_{X}\to T_{1}(F)(S)](-S)

which, for
T_{1}(F)\neq 0
, is a 1-dimensional
\mathrm{PT}
pair on
X
as in
[
PT1
]
.

Moreover, for
n=3
and an effective divisor
S\subset X
, this induces an equivalence between
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
pairs
(F,s)
on
X
with
S=\mathrm{Supp}(F)^{\mathrm{pure}}
and
T_{1}(F)\neq 0
, and
\mathrm{PT}
pairs on
X
.

Proof.

All but the last sentence were proved in
[
BKP
]
. For the last part, given a
\mathrm{PT}
pair
(G,t)
on
X
, its support
S
is a divisor, hence Cohen-Macaulay and we can form the push-out

\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{X}(-S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G(-S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{X}\sqcup_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(-S)}G(-S).}

Then the bottom row defines a
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
pair with torsion subsheaf
G(-S)
, and pure part of its support equal to
S
. This construction is easily seen to be the inverse of the construction in loc. cit.
∎

Proposition 2.6
.

For any
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
pair
(F,s)
on
X
with Gorenstein support
S
and non-zero cokernel
Q
, we have a natural quasi-isomorphism

F^{\vee}\otimes K_{X}[n-2]\cong[\mathcal{O}_{S}\to Q^{D}\otimes K_{S}^{\vee}]\otimes K_{S}

where
Q^{D}:=\curly Ext^{n-1}_{X}(Q,K_{X})
is a pure 1-dimensional sheaf scheme theoretically supported on
S
.
Moreover, this induces an equivalence between
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
pairs supported on
S
with non-zero cokernel and 1-dimensional
\mathrm{PT}
pairs on
S
.

Proposition 2.7
.

Let
\iota:S\hookrightarrow X
be a smooth surface in a quasi-projective variety of dimension
n
. A
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
pair
(F,s)
on
X
with support
S
and non-zero cokernel
Q
is equivalent to a pair
(C,Z)
of a (non-zero) effective divisor
C\subseteq S
and a 0-dimensional subscheme
Z\subseteq C
. Moreover,
Q\cong\jmath_{*}I_{Z/C}(C)
, where
\jmath:C\hookrightarrow X
denotes the inclusion.

Proof.

Except for the last sentence, this is proved in
[
BKP
, Sect. 2.3]
. By Proposition
2.6
, the stable pair
J^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}=[\mathcal{O}_{S}\to G]
corresponding to a
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
pair
(F,s)
with non-zero cokernel
Q
is obtained by
F^{\vee}\otimes K_{X}[n-2]\cong\iota_{*}(J^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\otimes K_{S})
. In particular, the cokernel
Q
satisfies
Q^{D}\cong\iota_{*}(G\otimes K_{S})
, i.e.,
Q^{\vee}\otimes K_{X}[n-1]\cong\iota_{*}(G\otimes K_{S})
by purity of
Q
. Denote the scheme theoretic support of
G
by
\jmath:C\hookrightarrow X
, then

\displaystyle(\iota_{*}(G\otimes K_{S}))^{\vee}\cong\jmath_{*}R\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{C}(G\otimes K_{S}|_{C},\jmath^{!}\mathcal{O}_{X})\cong(\jmath_{*}G^{\vee}(C))^{\vee}\otimes K_{X}^{-1}[1-n],

where we used
K_{C}\cong K_{S}|_{C}(C)
(in particular
C
is Gorenstein). Hence
Q\cong\jmath_{*}G^{\vee}(C)
. Since
C
is Gorenstein, we have
G^{\vee}\cong G^{*}\cong I_{Z/C}

[
PT3
, Lem. B.2]
and the result follows.
∎

2.2.
Moduli spaces and virtual structures

We now review the moduli spaces and virtual structures introduced in
[
BKP
]
with a few adaptations required for the quasi-projective case.
For smooth quasi-projective varieties, it is convenient to work on a “compactification”, so we can apply Serre duality.
Let
X
be a smooth quasi-projective variety. An open embedding
j:X\hookrightarrow\overline{X}
is a
good compactification
of
X
if
\overline{X}
is a smooth projective variety and
\overline{X}\setminus X
is a simple normal crossings divisor. Existence of good compactifications is guaranteed by Hironaka’s theorem.

We denote by
H^{*}_{c}(X,\mathbb{Q})
the singular cohomology groups with compact support (with respect to the complex analytic topology on
X
). Then there is a natural Chern character map

\operatorname{ch}:K_{0}(X)_{c}\to H^{*}_{c}(X,\mathbb{Q})\,.

Let
X
be a smooth quasi-projective 4-fold and denote by
{\curly Pair}(X)_{c}
the moduli stack of non-zero pairs
(F,s)
where
F\in\mathrm{Coh}(X)_{c}
and
0\neq s\in H^{0}(X,F)
. As above, we fix a good compactification
j:X\hookrightarrow\overline{X}
. For an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module
F
on
X
, let
j_{!}F
be the extension by zero. For compactly supported sheaves,
j_{!}
preserves coherence, i.e.,
j_{!}:\mathrm{Coh}(X)_{c}\to\mathrm{Coh}(\overline{X})
. This follows from the fact that for any object
F
of
\mathrm{Coh}(X)_{c}
, we have
j_{!}F\cong j_{*}F
.
We fix

\operatorname{ch}(F)=v:=(0,0,\gamma,\beta,n-\gamma\cdot\mathrm{td}_{2}(X))\in H^{*}_{c}(X,\mathbb{Q})\,.

Using a good compactification, the following proposition follows from
[
BKP
, Prop. 3.3, Thm. 3.10]
.

Proposition 2.8
.

For
q\in\{-1,0,1\}
and
v\in H^{*}_{c}(X,\mathbb{Q})
, there exists an open substack
{\curly P}^{(q)}_{v}(X)
of
{\curly Pair}(X)_{c}
for which the
\mathbb{C}
-points are given by

{\curly P}^{(q)}_{v}(X)(\mathbb{C})=\{(F,s):\operatorname{ch}(F)=v,\,(F,s)\text{ is a }\mathrm{PT}_{q}\text{ pair, }F\in\mathrm{Coh}(X)_{c}\}.

Then
{\curly P}^{(q)}_{v}(X)\to\curly P^{(q)}_{j_{*}v}(\overline{X})
,
(F,s)\mapsto(j_{*}F,s^{\prime})
, where
s^{\prime}
is the composition of the natural map
\mathcal{O}_{\overline{X}}\to j_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}
and
j_{*}s
, is an open embedding.
In particular,
{\curly P}^{(q)}_{v}(X)
is a quasi-projective scheme.

Suppose
X
is additionally Calabi-Yau and
let
\curly P:={\curly P}^{(q)}_{v}(X)
.
We consider the moduli stack
[
Ina
,
Lie
]

{\curly Perf}(\overline{X},1-j_{*}v)^{\mathrm{spl}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\overline{X}}}

of simple perfect complexes
I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
on
\overline{X}
with
\operatorname{ch}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})=1-j_{*}v
and
\det(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})\cong\mathcal{O}_{\overline{X}}
. Then Proposition
2.8
and
[
BKP
, Thm. 3.19]
imply:

Proposition 2.9
.

Let
j:X\to\overline{X}
be a good compactification. The composition

{\curly P}^{(q)}_{v}(X)\to\curly P^{(q)}_{j_{*}v}(\overline{X})\to{\curly Perf}(\overline{X},1-j_{*}v)^{\mathrm{spl}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\overline{X}}}\,,\,(F,s)\to[\mathcal{O}_{\overline{X}}\to j_{*}F]

is an open embedding.

As a consequence the moduli space
\curly P
has an obstruction theory

(7)

\phi:\mathbb{E}=R\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{\pi_{\curly P}}(\mathbb{I}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}},\mathbb{I}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})_{0}[3]\xrightarrow{\operatorname{At}(\mathbb{I}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})}\tau^{\geq-1}\mathbb{L}_{\curly P}

induced by the Atiyah class of the universal complex
\mathbb{I}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
on
X\times\curly P
, where
\pi_{\curly P}:X\times\curly P\to\curly P
denotes the projection, and
(\cdot)_{0}
denote the trace-free part. We will show this obstruction theory is 3-term and symmetric. This requires using Serre duality for which we need the good compactification.

Suppose
\mathbb{F}
,
\mathbb{G}
are coherent sheaves on
X\times B
flat over a base scheme
B
and with proper support. When
\mathbb{F}
has a section
s
, we have an induced complex
\mathbb{I}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}=[\mathcal{O}_{X\times B}\to\mathbb{F}]
. We write

\mathbb{I}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}_{\overline{X}}:=[\mathcal{O}_{\overline{X}\times B}\to(j\times 1_{B})_{*}\mathbb{F}]

for the composition of the adjunction unit
\mathcal{O}_{\overline{X}\times B}\to(j\times 1_{B})_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X\times B}
and
(j\times 1_{B})_{*}s
. Then
R\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{\overline{X}\times B}((j\times 1_{B})_{*}\mathbb{F},(j\times 1_{B})_{*}\mathbb{G})\cong(j\times 1_{B})_{*}R\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{X\times B}(\mathbb{F},\mathbb{G})
,
R\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{\overline{X}\times B}(\mathbb{I}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}_{\overline{X}},(j\times 1_{B})_{*}\mathbb{G})\cong(j\times 1_{B})_{*}R\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{X\times B}(\mathbb{I}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}},\mathbb{G})
,
R\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{\overline{X}\times B}(\mathbb{I}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}_{\overline{X}},\mathbb{I}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}_{\overline{X}})_{0}\cong(j\times 1_{B})_{*}R\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{X\times B}(\mathbb{I}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}},\mathbb{I}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})_{0}
. One can now apply Grothendieck-Verdier duality along the projective morphism
\pi_{B}:\overline{X}\times B\to B
in order to deduce

\displaystyle R\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{\pi_{B}}(\mathbb{F},\mathbb{G})\cong(R\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{\pi_{B}}(\mathbb{G},\mathbb{F}\otimes K_{X}[4])^{\vee},

\displaystyle R\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{\pi_{B}}(\mathbb{I}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}},\mathbb{G})\cong(R\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{\pi_{B}}(\mathbb{G},\mathbb{I}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\otimes K_{X}[4])^{\vee},

\displaystyle R\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{\pi_{B}}(\mathbb{I}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}},\mathbb{I}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})_{0}\cong(R\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{\pi_{B}}(\mathbb{I}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}},\mathbb{I}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\otimes K_{X}[4])_{0}^{\vee},

where we use
j^{*}K_{\overline{X}}\cong K_{X}
and for the last isomorphism we also use that we take the trace-free part.

Let
\overline{\curly P}:=\curly P^{(q)}_{j_{*}v}(\overline{X})
be the moduli space of
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pairs on the good compactification
\overline{X}
. Then it is straight-forward to show the following:

Proposition 2.10
.

Let
X
be a Calabi-Yau 4-fold. Let
j:X\to\overline{X}
be a good compactification. Denote by
\overline{\mathbb{I}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
the universal pair on
\overline{X}\times\overline{\curly P}
and consider the open embedding
\curly P\subset\overline{\curly P}
of Proposition
2.8
.
Then
\phi
in (
7
) is the restriction to
\curly P
of the 3-term obstruction theory

\overline{\phi}:\overline{\mathbb{E}}=R\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{\pi_{\overline{\curly P}}}(\overline{\mathbb{I}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}},\overline{\mathbb{I}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})_{0}[3]\xrightarrow{\operatorname{At}(\overline{\mathbb{I}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})}\tau^{\geq-1}\mathbb{L}_{\overline{\curly P}}.

Therefore
\phi
is 3-term and Grothendieck-Verdier duality for
\overline{\mathbb{E}}
induces a pairing
\theta:\mathbb{E}^{\vee}[2]\cong\mathbb{E}
satisfying
\theta=\theta^{\vee}[2]
. Hence
\phi
defines a 3-term
symmetric
obstruction theory.

Unlike the case of perfect (i.e., 2-term) obstruction theories, we require an additional property in order to construct the virtual cycle and virtual structure sheaf. Specifically, the intrinsic normal cone
\mathfrak{C}_{\curly P}
should vanish under the quadratic function on
\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{E}):=h^{1}/h^{0}(\mathbb{E}^{\vee})
induced by the symmetric pairing
[
OT
,
Par
]
. This property follows from the existence of a
(-2)
-shifted symplectic derived enhancement of
\curly P
combined with
[
BBJ
]
. The construction of
(-2)
-shifted symplectic structures was carried out in
[
PTVV
]
in the projective case and in
[
Pre
]
in the quasi-projective case.

Therefore, combining Proposition
2.10
with the work of Oh-Thomas
[
OT
]
, for any choice of orientation, we obtain a virtual cycle and structure sheaf

\displaystyle[\curly P]^{\mathrm{vir}}\in A_{\mathrm{vd}}(\curly P),\quad\mathrm{vd}:=n-\frac{1}{2}(j_{*}\gamma)^{2}

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\curly P}^{\mathrm{vir}}\in K_{0}(\curly P,\mathbb{Z}[\tfrac{1}{2}]).

This virtual cycle is a lift of the Borisov-Joyce virtual cycle
[
BJ
]
(constructed by Cao-Leung
[
CL
]
in special cases) from homology groups to Chow groups.
The existence of orientations in the projective case was shown in
[
CGJ
]
and in the quasi-projective case in
[
Boj1
]
.

The number
\mathrm{vd}
is sometimes non-integer, in which case we define
A_{\mathrm{vd}}(\curly P)
to be zero (notation and conventions). The following simple observation implies integrality of
\mathrm{vd}
:

Lemma 2.11
.

Suppose
\gamma=\sum_{i}m_{i}[S_{i}]
where
m_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}
and
S_{i}\subset X
are irreducible smooth projective surfaces for which
N_{S_{i}/X}
splits. Then
\mathrm{vd}\in\mathbb{Z}
. In particular, for
X
a toric Calabi-Yau 4-fold and any
\gamma\in H^{4}_{c}(X,\mathbb{Q})
Poincaré dual to an integral algebraic cycle, we have
\mathrm{vd}\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Proof.

It suffices to show that
(j_{*}[S_{i}])^{2}
is even. By assumption,
N_{S_{i}/X}\cong L_{1}\oplus L_{2}
, where
L_{1}\otimes L_{2}\cong K_{S}
since
X
is Calabi-Yau. Hence
(j_{*}[S_{i}])^{2}=\int_{S_{i}}c_{2}(N_{S_{i}/X})=L_{1}\cdot(K_{S}-L_{1})=2(\chi(\mathcal{O}_{S_{i}})-\chi(L_{1}))
. The last statement of the lemma follows because any such
\gamma
is Poincaré dual to an integral linear combination of
T_{X}
-fixed irreducible smooth projective surfaces
S_{i}\subset X
, and
N_{S_{i}/X}
splits for any such
S_{i}
.
∎

2.2.1.
Equivariant case

Finally, let
X
be a Calabi-Yau 4-fold endowed with the action of an algebraic torus
T
preserving the Calabi-Yau volume form. For example, if
X
is a toric Calabi-Yau 4-fold, then it has an action of
T_{X}\cong(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{4}
, which contains a 3-dimensional subtorus preserving the Calabi-Yau volume form. Note that a toric Calabi-Yau 4-fold is never projective.

Assume there exists a
T
-equivariant good compactification, i.e., an irreducible smooth projective 4-fold
\overline{X}
with
T
-action and a
T
-equivariant open embedding
j:X\hookrightarrow\overline{X}
such that
\overline{X}\setminus X
is a simple normal crossings divisor.
For example, a
T
-equivariant good compactification always exist for a toric Calabi-Yau 4-fold by turning its fan into a complete fan by adding sufficiently many 4-dimensional rational simplicial cones.
4
4

4

Any smooth quasi-projective toric variety has a toric completion to a projective toric variety
[
Sum
]
, which in turn has a toric projective resolution
[
CLS
, Thm. 11.1.9]
.

The results of this section hold
T
-equivariantly using the
T
-equivariant Atiyah class
[
Ric
]
.
The upshot is that we obtain classes

\displaystyle[\curly P]^{\mathrm{vir}}\in A_{\mathrm{vd}}^{T}(\curly P),\quad\mathrm{vd}:=n-\frac{1}{2}(j_{*}\gamma)^{2}

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\curly P}^{\mathrm{vir}}\in K_{0}^{T}(\curly P,\mathbb{Z}[\tfrac{1}{2}]).

2.3.
Virtual localization

In this section, we discuss the Oh-Thomas localization formula
[
OT
]
, with special focus on the case when the fixed locus is isolated reduced.

Let
M
be a quasi-projective scheme with 3-term symmetric obstruction theory
\phi:\mathbb{E}\to\tau^{\geq-1}\mathbb{L}_{M}
.
Suppose
\phi
is oriented and isotropic. Let
T
be an algebraic torus acting on
M
and suppose
\phi
and the orientation are
T
-equivariant. Furthermore, suppose
\operatorname{rk}(\mathbb{E})=2\mathrm{vd}\in 2\mathbb{Z}
, then
5
5

5

If
\operatorname{rk}(\mathbb{E})
is odd, then the virtual cycle and virtual structure sheaf are zero.

[M]^{\mathrm{vir}}\in A_{\mathrm{vd}}^{T}(M),\quad\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{M}^{\mathrm{vir}}\in K_{0}^{T}(M,\mathbb{Z}[\tfrac{1}{2}]).

We will sometimes write
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{vir}}
instead of
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{M}^{\mathrm{vir}}
.

Let
\iota:M^{T}\hookrightarrow M
denote the inclusion of the fixed point locus. The (general) localization formula of Thomason
[
Tho
, Thm. 2.1]
, see also
[
Oko
, Sect. 2.3.1]
, states

\iota_{*}:K_{0}^{T}(M^{T})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[t,t^{-1}]}\mathbb{Z}(t)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\rightarrow}}K_{0}^{T}(M)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[t,t^{-1}]}\mathbb{Z}(t),

where
t
denotes the equivariant parameters of
T
and

K_{0}^{T}(M^{T})\cong K_{0}(M^{T})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}[t,t^{-1}].

Suppose
M^{T}
is
proper
. Then we can use this isomorphism to define

\chi(M,\alpha),\quad\alpha\in K_{0}^{T}(M)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[t,t^{-1}]}\mathbb{Z}(t)

by
\chi(M^{T},\widetilde{\alpha})
, where
\widetilde{\alpha}\in K_{0}^{T}(M^{T})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[t,t^{-1}]}\mathbb{Z}(t)
is the unique class satisfying
\iota_{*}\widetilde{\alpha}=\alpha
. In general this procedure is not explicit. Fortunately, for
\alpha=\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{M}^{\mathrm{vir}}
we have the explicit virtual localization formula of Oh-Thomas, which we will now discuss. Their formula requires taking square roots of the equivariant parameters
t

[
OT
, Sect. 7]
, so we will work with

\iota_{*}:K_{0}^{T}(M^{T})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[t,t^{-1}]}\mathbb{Q}(t^{\frac{1}{2}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\rightarrow}}K_{0}^{T}(M)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[t,t^{-1}]}\mathbb{Q}(t^{\frac{1}{2}})=:K_{0}^{T}(M)_{\mathrm{loc}}.

Theorem 2.12

(Oh-Thomas)
.

In the above setup, and denoting the connected components of
M^{T}
by
M_{i}
, we have

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{M}^{\mathrm{vir}}=\sum_{i}\iota_{*}\Bigg(\frac{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{M_{i}}^{\mathrm{vir}}}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{e}}(N^{\mathrm{vir}}|_{M_{i}})}\Bigg).

Let us explain this formula (see
[
OT
, Sect. 7]
for further details). On each component
M_{i}
, we have a decomposition into fixed and moving part

\mathbb{E}|_{M_{i}}=\mathbb{E}|_{M_{i}}^{f}\oplus\mathbb{E}|_{M_{i}}^{m}.

We refer to
N^{\mathrm{vir}}=\mathbb{E}^{\vee}|_{M^{T}}^{m}
as the virtual normal bundle. The complex
\mathbb{E}
is
T
-equivariantly quasi-isomorphic to a
T
-equivariant self-dual complex

\{F\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a}}{{\to}}E\cong E^{*}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a^{*}}}{{\to}}F^{*}\},

where
E
is a
T
-equivariant vector bundle with
T
-invariant
non-degenerate quadratic form
q:E\cong E^{*}
. The induced map
[
GP
,
CKL
]

\mathbb{E}|_{M_{i}}^{f}\to(\tau^{\geq-1}\mathbb{L}_{M})|_{M_{i}}^{f}\to\tau^{\geq-1}\mathbb{L}_{M_{i}}

is a 3-term obtruction theory.
Moreover, we see that
(N^{\mathrm{vir}}|_{M_{i}})^{\vee}
,
\mathbb{E}|_{M_{i}}^{f}
have self-dual resolutions

\displaystyle(N^{\mathrm{vir}}|_{M_{i}})^{\vee}

\displaystyle\cong\{F|_{M_{i}}^{m}\to E|_{M_{i}}^{m}\to F^{*}|_{M_{i}}^{m}\},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}|_{M_{i}}^{f}

\displaystyle\cong\{F|_{M_{i}}^{f}\to E|_{M_{i}}^{f}\to F^{*}|_{M_{i}}^{f}\}.

Choosing a generic 1-parameter subgroup
\mathbb{C}^{*}\leq T
, the virtual normal bundle
N^{\mathrm{vir}}|_{M_{i}}
can be oriented as follows. Serre duality provides a pairing between terms with weights
w>0
and terms with weights
w<0
, which gives a splitting of
N^{\mathrm{vir}}|_{M_{i}}
. Since we oriented
\mathbb{E}
and
N^{\mathrm{vir}}|_{M_{i}}
, we obtain an induced orientation for
\mathbb{E}|_{M_{i}}^{f}
and we obtain objects
[M_{i}]^{\mathrm{vir}}
,
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{M_{i}}^{\mathrm{vir}}
. Finally, we have

\sqrt{\mathfrak{e}}(N^{\mathrm{vir}}|_{M_{i}})=\frac{\widehat{\Lambda}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}(F^{*}|^{m}_{M_{i}})}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{e}}(E|_{M_{i}}^{m})},

where
\sqrt{\mathfrak{e}}(E|_{M_{i}}^{m})
is the
K
-theoretic square root Euler class defined in
[
OT
]
. If
\Lambda
is a positively oriented maximal isotropic subbundle of
E|_{M_{i}}^{m}
, then
\sqrt{\mathfrak{e}}(E|^{m}_{M_{i}})=\widehat{\Lambda}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\Lambda^{*}
and we have

\sqrt{\mathfrak{e}}(N^{\mathrm{vir}}|_{M_{i}})=\widehat{\Lambda}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}(F^{*}|_{M_{i}}^{m}-\Lambda^{*}).

The following proposition was first proved in
[
KR
]
.

Proposition 2.13
.

Suppose
M^{T}
consists of isolated reduced points. Then

2\mathrm{vd}(\{P\})=\operatorname{rk}(\mathbb{E}|_{P}^{f})\leq 0,\quad\forall P\in M^{T}.

Moreover

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{M}^{\mathrm{vir}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in M^{T}\\
\mathrm{vd}(\{P\})=0\end{subarray}}(-1)^{\sigma_{P}}\iota_{*}\Bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{e}}(\mathbb{E}^{\vee}|_{P})}\Bigg),

where
(-1)^{\sigma_{P}}
is the orientation on
\{P\}
induced by the globally chosen orientation on
M
and

(8)

(\sqrt{\mathfrak{e}}(\mathbb{E}^{\vee}|_{P}))^{2}=(-1)^{\frac{\operatorname{rk}(\mathbb{E})}{2}}\widehat{\Lambda}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}(\mathbb{E}|_{P}).

Proof.

Consider the complex

(9)

\mathbb{E}|_{P}^{f}\cong\{F|^{f}_{P}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a}}{{\to}}E|^{f}_{P}\cong E^{*}|^{f}_{P}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a^{*}}}{{\to}}F^{*}|^{f}_{P}\}.

By reducedness, the second map is surjective and, consequently, the first map is injective. Moreover
F|^{f}_{P}\hookrightarrow E|^{f}_{P}
is isotropic so
\dim_{\mathbb{C}}(F|^{f}_{P})\leq\dim_{\mathbb{C}}(E|^{f}_{P})/2
and hence
\mathrm{vd}(\{P\})\leq 0
.

When
\mathrm{vd}(\{P\})<0
, we have
[\{P\}]^{\mathrm{vir}}=0
. Suppose
\mathrm{vd}(\{P\})=0
, then
F|^{f}_{P}\hookrightarrow E|^{f}_{P}
is maximal isotropic and (
9
) is acyclic, i.e.,
\mathbb{E}|_{P}^{f}\cong 0
. Hence
[\{P\}]^{\mathrm{vir}}=\pm\{P\}
, where the sign is determined by the choice of orientation.
Moreover,
N^{\mathrm{vir}}|_{P}=\mathbb{E}^{\vee}|_{P}
(as classes in
K_{0}^{T}(\mathrm{pt})
) and (
8
) follows from the definition of
K
-theoretic square root Euler class
[
OT
, Prop. 5.4]
.
∎

Remark 2.14
.

In
[
KR
]
, the following formula for the sign is derived when the orientation on
M
is induced by a choice of maximal isotropic subbundle
\Lambda\subset E
. For
P\in M^{T}
such that
\mathrm{vd}(\{P\})=0
, choose a splitting
E|_{P}=\Lambda|_{P}\oplus\Lambda^{*}|_{P}
and denote projection by
p_{\Lambda}:E|_{P}\to\Lambda|_{P}
. Then

\sigma_{P}\equiv\dim_{\mathbb{C}}\Big(\operatorname{coker}(p_{\Lambda}\circ a)^{f}\Big)\mod 2,

where
a:F|^{f}_{P}\to E|^{f}_{P}
as before. For the computations in this paper, we do not have an explicit self-dual resolution (
9
), so this formula is not enough to determine the sign.

3.
Invariants and correspondences

In this section, we define the virtual invariants which form the subject of this paper and we derive some of their structural properties. We also formulate conjectural
K
-theoretic
\mathrm{DT}
–
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
correspondences. We first introduce all invariants in the projective case and then in the
T
-equivariant quasi-projective setup.

3.1.
Tautological invariants

Let
X
be a projective Calabi-Yau 4-fold and fix a Chern character

v=(0,0,\gamma,\beta,n-\gamma\cdot\mathrm{td}_{2}(X))\in H^{*}(X,\mathbb{Q}).

We consider any of the
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
moduli spaces
\curly P:={\curly P}^{(q)}_{v}(X)
and we denote the universal pair by

\mathbb{I}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}=[\mathcal{O}_{X\times\curly P}\to\mathbb{F}].

Consider the projections

\textstyle{X\times\curly P\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{\curly P}}
\textstyle{X}
\textstyle{\curly P.}

Fixing a line bundle
L\in\mathrm{Pic}(X)
, we are interested in the following tautological complex

(10)

R\pi_{\curly P*}(\mathbb{F}\otimes L),\quad\operatorname{rk}:=\operatorname{rk}(R\pi_{\curly P*}(\mathbb{F}\otimes L))=(\tfrac{1}{2}L\gamma+\beta)L+n,

where we suppress the pull-back of
L
from
X
to
X\times\curly P
. We endow
\curly P
with a trivial
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-action for which we denote a corresponding primitive character by
y
. We denote its equivariant Euler class by
m=c_{1}^{\mathbb{C}^{*}}(y)
. Writing
(R\pi_{\curly P*}(\mathbb{F}\otimes L))^{\vee}=V_{0}-V_{1}\in K^{0}(\curly P)
, where
V_{0},V_{1}
are classes of vector bundles, we can therefore consider the equivariant Euler class

e((R\pi_{\curly P*}(\mathbb{F}\otimes L))^{\vee}\otimes y):=\frac{e(V_{0}\otimes y)}{e(V_{1}\otimes y)}\in A^{*}(\curly P)[m,m^{-1}],

where

(11)

e(V_{i}\otimes y)=c_{\operatorname{rk}V_{i}}(V_{i})+c_{\operatorname{rk}V_{i}-1}(V_{i})m+\cdots+m^{\operatorname{rk}(V_{i})}.

By
[
OT
]
and
[
BKP
, Thm. 3.19]
, we have a 3-term symmetric obstruction theory (
7
)
and we define its virtual tangent bundle by
T_{\curly P}^{\mathrm{vir}}:=\mathbb{E}^{\vee}
. Fixing an orientation, we obtain a virtual cycle

\displaystyle[\curly P]^{\mathrm{vir}}\in A_{\mathrm{vd}}(\curly P),\quad\mathrm{vd}:=n-\frac{1}{2}\gamma^{2}.

Definition 3.1
.

We define
tautological
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
invariants
of
X
by

\displaystyle\langle L\rangle^{\mathrm{PT}_{q}}_{X,v}:=\int_{[\curly P]^{\mathrm{vir}}}e((R\pi_{\curly P*}(\mathbb{F}\otimes L))^{\vee}\otimes y)\in\mathbb{Z}\cdot m^{\operatorname{rk}-\mathrm{vd}},

where
\operatorname{rk}-\mathrm{vd}=\frac{1}{2}\gamma(\gamma+L^{2})+\beta L
. We also write
\langle L\rangle^{\mathrm{PT}_{q}}_{X,\gamma,\beta,n}
for this invariant.

Remark 3.2
.

By (
11
), we have

\langle L\rangle^{\mathrm{PT}_{q}}_{X,v}=m^{\operatorname{rk}-\mathrm{vd}}\int_{[\curly P]^{\mathrm{vir}}}c((R\pi_{\curly P*}(\mathbb{F}\otimes L))^{\vee}),

where
c(\cdot)
denotes the total Chern class.

It is also reasonable to consider descendent insertions

\tau_{k}(\sigma):=\pi_{\curly P*}\Big(\pi_{X}^{*}\sigma\cap\operatorname{ch}_{2+k}(\mathbb{F})\Big)\in H^{2(a+k-2)}(\curly P,\mathbb{Q}),\quad\sigma\in H^{2a}(X,\mathbb{Q})

and study
descendent
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
invariants
of
X

\langle\tau_{k_{1}}(\sigma_{1})\cdots\tau_{k_{\ell}}(\sigma_{\ell})\rangle_{X}^{\mathrm{PT}_{q}}:=\int_{[\curly P]^{\mathrm{vir}}}\tau_{k_{1}}(\sigma_{1})\cdots\tau_{k_{\ell}}(\sigma_{\ell}).

We do not pursue descendent invariants in this paper.

3.2.
K
-theoretic invariants

We continue using the notation from the previous section. We consider the virtual structure sheaf

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\curly P}^{\mathrm{vir}}\in K_{0}(\curly P,\mathbb{Z}[\tfrac{1}{2}]).

Recall the notation for
\widehat{\Lambda}^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}(\cdot)
from the introduction. We are interested in the
symmetrized equivariant
K
-theoretic Euler class
of the tautological complex

(12)

\widehat{\Lambda}^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}(R\pi_{\curly P*}(\mathbb{F}\otimes L)\otimes y^{-1})^{\vee}=(-1)^{\operatorname{rk}}\cdot\widehat{\Lambda}^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}(R\pi_{\curly P*}(\mathbb{F}\otimes L)\otimes y^{-1}).

More precisely, writing
R\pi_{\curly P*}(\mathbb{F}\otimes L)=V_{0}-V_{1}
as before, we have

\widehat{\Lambda}^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}(R\pi_{\curly P*}(\mathbb{F}\otimes L)\otimes y^{-1})=y^{\frac{\operatorname{rk}}{2}}\frac{\det(V_{1})^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\det(V_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\frac{\Lambda_{-y^{-1}}V_{0}}{\Lambda_{-y^{-1}}V_{1}}\in K^{0}(\curly P,\mathbb{Z}[\tfrac{1}{2}])(\!(y^{-\frac{1}{2}})\!).

Note that identity (
12
) only makes sense when the left hand side is expanded as formal power series in
y^{-1}
.

Definition 3.3
.

We define
K
-theoretic invariants
of
X
by

\displaystyle\langle\!\langle L\rangle\!\rangle^{\mathrm{PT}_{q}}_{X,v}

\displaystyle=\chi(\curly P,\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\curly P}\otimes\widehat{\Lambda}^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}(R\pi_{\curly P*}(\mathbb{F}\otimes L)\otimes y^{-1}))\in\mathbb{Q}(\!(y^{-\frac{1}{2}})\!).

We also write
\langle\!\langle L\rangle\!\rangle^{\mathrm{PT}_{q}}_{X,\gamma,\beta,n}
for this invariant.

Recall that for any formal power series
f(x)
over a commutative
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra
R
such that
f(0)=1
, we can consider the formal power series

\sqrt{f}(x):=f^{\frac{1}{2}}(x):=e^{\frac{1}{2}\log f(x)}\in 1+xR[[x]].

It has the properties
(f^{\frac{1}{2}})^{2}=(f^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}=f
and
f^{\frac{1}{2}}g^{\frac{1}{2}}=(fg)^{\frac{1}{2}}
. An example relevant to us is
R=\mathbb{Q}
and
f(x)=x/(1-e^{-x})
, which appears in the definition of the Todd class, i.e., for a line bundle
L
we have

\mathrm{td}(L)=\frac{c_{1}(L)}{1-e^{-c_{1}(L)}}.

We start with a general property which states that, at the level of virtual invariants, the moduli spaces
\curly P
are themselves
virtually Calabi–Yau
.
6
6

6

The results of this section hold for any moduli space endowed with 3-term symmetric isotropic obstruction theory.

Proposition 3.4
.

For
V\in K^{0}(\curly P)
, we have

\chi(\curly P,\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\curly P}\otimes V)=(-1)^{\mathrm{vd}}\chi(\curly P,\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\curly P}\otimes V^{\vee}).

Proof.

By
[
OT
, Thm. 6.1]
we have

\chi(\curly P,\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\curly P}\otimes V)=\int_{[\curly P]^{\mathrm{vir}}}\operatorname{ch}(V)\sqrt{\mathrm{td}}(T_{\curly P}^{\mathrm{vir}})\,.

On the other hand, using
T^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\curly P}[2]\cong(T_{\curly P}^{\mathrm{vir}})^{\vee}
, we have

\chi(\curly P,\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\curly P}\otimes V^{\vee})=\int_{[\curly P]^{\mathrm{vir}}}\operatorname{ch}(V^{\vee})\sqrt{\mathrm{td}}((T_{\curly P}^{\mathrm{vir}})^{\vee})\,.

Therefore, the second intersection number is obtained from the first by replacing all Chern roots
x
by
-x
and the result follows.
∎

Remark 3.5
.

This lemma provides an indication why
\chi(\curly P,\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\curly P})
is a bad invariant to consider: it is automatically zero when
\mathrm{vd}
is odd. In fact, Bojko
[
Boj2
]
showed (assuming a certain conjectural vertex algebra wall-crossing framework for virtual classes similar to
[
GJT
,
Joy
]
) that for Hilbert schemes of points

\chi(\operatorname{Hilb}^{n}(X),\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\operatorname{Hilb}^{n}(X)})=0,

for all
n
.

A priori, the
K
-theoretic invariants are Laurent series in
y^{-\frac{1}{2}}
. However, more can be said about their structure. We define

[y]:=y^{\frac{1}{2}}-y^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

A polynomial
f(y)
of degree
n
is called
palindromic
if
f(y)=y^{n}f(y^{-1})
.

Proposition 3.6
.

There exists a Laurent polynomial
f(y)\in\mathbb{Q}[y^{\frac{1}{2}},y^{-\frac{1}{2}}]
such that
y^{\frac{\mathrm{vd}}{2}}f(y)
is a palindromic polynomial of degree
\leq\mathrm{vd}
and

\langle\!\langle L\rangle\!\rangle_{X,v}^{\mathrm{PT}_{q}}=[y]^{\operatorname{rk}-\mathrm{vd}}f(y).

Proof.

For a line bundle
L
, we have

\operatorname{ch}(\widehat{\Lambda}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}(L\otimes y^{-1}))=y^{\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\frac{c_{1}(L)}{2}}-y^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{\frac{c_{1}(L)}{2}}.

Let us write
R\pi_{\curly P*}(\mathbb{F}\otimes L)=V_{0}-V_{1}
and
T_{\curly P}^{\mathrm{vir}}=E_{0}-E_{1}
, where
V_{0},V_{1},E_{0},E_{1}
are vector bundles with Chern roots
\{v_{i}\},\{w_{j}\},\{x_{k}\},\{u_{\ell}\}
respectively. Then

(13)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\langle\!\langle L\rangle\!\rangle_{X,v}^{\mathrm{PT}_{q}}=\int_{[\curly P]^{\mathrm{vir}}}\Phi(y)\cdot\Bigg(\frac{\prod_{k}\frac{x_{k}}{1-e^{-x_{k}}}}{\prod_{\ell}\frac{u_{\ell}}{1-e^{-u_{\ell}}}}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}},\\
&\Phi(y):=\frac{\prod_{i}(y^{\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\frac{v_{i}}{2}}-y^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{\frac{v_{i}}{2}})}{\prod_{j}(y^{\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\frac{w_{j}}{2}}-y^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{\frac{w_{j}}{2}})}.\end{split}

We rewrite
\Phi(y)
as follows

\Phi(y)=[y]^{\operatorname{rk}}\frac{\prod_{i}\Big(e^{\frac{v_{i}}{2}}-\frac{y^{\frac{1}{2}}}{[y]}(e^{\frac{v_{i}}{2}}-e^{-\frac{v_{i}}{2}})\Big)}{\prod_{j}\Big(e^{\frac{w_{j}}{2}}-\frac{y^{\frac{1}{2}}}{[y]}(e^{\frac{w_{j}}{2}}-e^{-\frac{w_{j}}{2}})\Big)}.

Note that

e^{\frac{v_{i}}{2}}-\frac{y^{\frac{1}{2}}}{[y]}(e^{\frac{v_{i}}{2}}-e^{-\frac{v_{i}}{2}})=1+\ldots-\frac{y^{\frac{1}{2}}}{[y]}(v_{i}+\ldots),

where
\ldots
are terms in
A^{\geq 1}(\curly P)
and similarly for the terms involving
w_{j}
in the denominator of
\Phi(y)
. Therefore, we can write

\Phi(y)=[y]^{\operatorname{rk}}\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}[y]^{-p}y^{\frac{p}{2}}\Phi_{p},

where
\Phi_{p}\in A^{\geq p}(\curly P)
. Combined with (
13
), where we integrate over a class of degree
\mathrm{vd}
, we obtain the proposition except for palindromicity.

For the palindromic property, we consider

\int_{[\curly P]^{\mathrm{vir}}}\Phi(y^{-1})\cdot\Bigg(\frac{\prod_{k}\frac{x_{k}}{1-e^{-x_{k}}}}{\prod_{\ell}\frac{u_{\ell}}{1-e^{-u_{\ell}}}}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}

and use a similar strategy to the proof of Proposition
3.4
. Indeed

\displaystyle\int_{[\curly P]^{\mathrm{vir}}}\Phi(y^{-1})\Bigg(\frac{\prod_{k}\frac{x_{k}}{1-e^{-x_{k}}}}{\prod_{\ell}\frac{u_{\ell}}{1-e^{-u_{\ell}}}}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}=(-1)^{\operatorname{rk}}\int_{[\curly P]^{\mathrm{vir}}}\frac{\prod_{i}(y^{\frac{1}{2}}e^{\frac{v_{i}}{2}}-y^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\frac{v_{i}}{2}})}{\prod_{j}(y^{\frac{1}{2}}e^{\frac{w_{j}}{2}}-y^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\frac{w_{j}}{2}})}\Bigg(\frac{\prod_{k}\frac{x_{k}}{1-e^{-x_{k}}}}{\prod_{\ell}\frac{u_{\ell}}{1-e^{-u_{\ell}}}}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\operatorname{rk}}\int_{[\curly P]^{\mathrm{vir}}}\frac{\prod_{i}(y^{\frac{1}{2}}e^{\frac{v_{i}}{2}}-y^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\frac{v_{i}}{2}})}{\prod_{j}(y^{\frac{1}{2}}e^{\frac{w_{j}}{2}}-y^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\frac{w_{j}}{2}})}\Bigg(\frac{\prod_{k}\frac{-x_{k}}{1-e^{x_{k}}}}{\prod_{\ell}\frac{-u_{\ell}}{1-e^{u_{\ell}}}}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}},

where we used
\det(T_{\curly P}^{\mathrm{vir}})^{\otimes 2}\cong\mathcal{O}_{\curly P}
, which implies
\prod_{k}e^{2x_{k}}\prod_{\ell}e^{-2u_{\ell}}=1
. Thus the integrand is obtained from (
13
) by simultaneously replacing all
v_{i},w_{j},x_{k},u_{\ell}
by
-v_{i},-w_{j},-x_{k},-u_{\ell}
. Hence

\displaystyle\int_{[\curly P]^{\mathrm{vir}}}\Phi(y^{-1})\cdot\Bigg(\frac{\prod_{k}\frac{x_{k}}{1-e^{-x_{k}}}}{\prod_{\ell}\frac{u_{\ell}}{1-e^{-u_{\ell}}}}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}=(-1)^{\operatorname{rk}+\mathrm{vd}}\int_{[\curly P]^{\mathrm{vir}}}\Phi(y)\cdot\Bigg(\frac{\prod_{k}\frac{x_{k}}{1-e^{-x_{k}}}}{\prod_{\ell}\frac{u_{\ell}}{1-e^{-u_{\ell}}}}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}},

which implies the palindromic property of
f
.
∎

The
K
-theoretic invariants are refinements of the tautological invariants.

Proposition 3.7
.

We have

\langle L\rangle_{X,v}^{\mathrm{PT}_{q}}=m^{\operatorname{rk}-\mathrm{vd}}\cdot\lim_{y\to 1}[y]^{\mathrm{vd}-\operatorname{rk}}\langle\!\langle L\rangle\!\rangle_{X,v}^{\mathrm{PT}_{q}}.

Proof.

By the definition of
\Phi(y),\Phi_{p}
in the proof of Proposition
3.6
, we have

\lim_{y\to 1}[y]^{\mathrm{vd}-\operatorname{rk}}\langle\!\langle L\rangle\!\rangle_{X,v}^{\mathrm{PT}_{q}}=\int_{[\curly P]^{\mathrm{vir}}}\Phi_{\mathrm{vd}},

where the square root Todd class does not contribute since
\deg[\curly P]^{\mathrm{vir}}=\mathrm{vd}
. The desired result is

\int_{[\curly P]^{\mathrm{vir}}}\mathrm{Coeff}_{z^{\mathrm{vd}}}\Bigg(\frac{\prod_{i}\Big(e^{\frac{v_{i}}{2}}-z(e^{\frac{v_{i}}{2}}-e^{-\frac{v_{i}}{2}})\Big)}{\prod_{j}\Big(e^{\frac{w_{j}}{2}}-z(e^{\frac{w_{j}}{2}}-e^{-\frac{w_{j}}{2}})\Big)}\Bigg)=\int_{[\curly P]^{\mathrm{vir}}}\mathrm{Coeff}_{z^{\mathrm{vd}}}\Bigg(\frac{\prod_{i}(1-zv_{i})}{\prod_{j}(1-zw_{j})}\Bigg),

where the equality follows from the fact that
\deg[\curly P]^{\mathrm{vir}}=\mathrm{vd}
. The right hand side is precisely

\int_{[\curly P]^{\mathrm{vir}}}c((R\pi_{\curly P*}(\mathbb{F}\otimes L))^{\vee}),

which implies the result by Remark
3.2

∎

Remark 3.8
.

Suppose we have a self-dual resolution
\mathbb{E}\cong[F\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a}}{{\to}}E\cong E^{*}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a^{*}}}{{\to}}F^{*}]
such that
\operatorname{rk}(E)
is even and
a(F)\subset\Lambda
for some maximal isotropic subbundle
\Lambda
. Suppose furthermore that
\phi
factors through the quotient
(\frac{1}{2}T_{\curly P}^{\mathrm{vir}})^{\vee}:=[\Lambda^{*}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a^{*}}}{{\to}}F^{*}]
.
Examples are when
\phi
arises as a
(-2)
-shifted cotangent bundle of a scheme with perfect obstruction theory
[
OT
, Sect. 8]
. Then
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\curly P}\otimes(\det(\frac{1}{2}T_{\curly P}^{\mathrm{vir}}))^{\frac{1}{2}}\in K^{0}(\curly P)
, i.e., it is an
integral

K
-theory class by
[
OT
, eqn. (105)]
. Thus, if furthermore there exists a line bundle
\curly L
on
\curly P
such that

(\det(\tfrac{1}{2}T_{\curly P}^{\mathrm{vir}})\otimes\det(R\pi_{\curly P*}(\mathbb{F}\otimes L)))^{\frac{1}{2}}=\curly L\in K^{0}(\curly P),

then the coefficients of the Laurent polynomial in Proposition
3.6
are integer (note that
[y]^{\operatorname{rk}-\mathrm{vd}}
is an invertible element of
\mathbb{Z}(\!(y^{-\frac{1}{2}})\!)
and its inverse has integer coefficients). This integrality is explored further in the sequels to this paper.

3.3.
\mathrm{DT}
–
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
correspondence

We continue using the notation from the previous sections. We recall the three types of surface objects on
X
:
\mathrm{DT}:=\mathrm{PT}_{-1}
,
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
,
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
(Definition
2.1
). We note that
\operatorname{Hilb}^{0}(X)=\{\varnothing\}
, in which case
\operatorname{rk}=0
by (
10
), so
\langle\!\langle L\rangle\!\rangle_{X,0,0,0}^{\mathrm{DT}}=1
(for appropriate orientation).

Our main conjecture is as follows.

Conjecture 3.9
.

Let
X
be a projective Calabi-Yau 4-fold and
L
a line bundle on
X
. For any
\gamma\in H^{4}(X,\mathbb{Q})
and
\beta\in H^{6}(X,\mathbb{Q})
, there exist orientations such that

\frac{\sum_{n}\langle\!\langle L\rangle\!\rangle_{X,\gamma,\beta,n}^{\mathrm{DT}}q^{n}}{\sum_{n}\langle\!\langle L\rangle\!\rangle_{X,0,0,n}^{\mathrm{DT}}q^{n}}=\sum_{n}\langle\!\langle L\rangle\!\rangle_{X,\gamma,\beta,n}^{\mathrm{PT}_{0}}q^{n}.

In fact, for fixed
\gamma,\beta
, the
\mathrm{DT}
and
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
moduli spaces are empty for
n\ll 0

[
BKP
, Prop. 2.14]
. Therefore, the series in the conjecture are all Laurent series in
q
. There are choices of
\gamma,\beta
such that all
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
pairs
(F,s)
on
X
with
\operatorname{ch}_{2}(F)=\gamma
and
\operatorname{ch}_{3}(F)=\beta
are also
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
pairs, and vice versa. In this case, the
\mathrm{PT}_{0}=\mathrm{PT}_{1}
moduli spaces are empty for
n\gg 0

[
BKP
, Prop. 2.14]
. Then the right hand side of the formula is a Laurent
polynomial
in
q
.

Using Proposition
3.7
, and the fact that
\operatorname{rk}-\mathrm{vd}=\frac{1}{2}\gamma(\gamma+L^{2})+\beta L
is independent of
n
, we obtain the following corollary.

Corollary 3.10
.

Assume Conjecture
3.9
holds. Let
X
be a projective Calabi-Yau 4-fold and
L
a line bundle on
X
. For any
\gamma\in H^{4}(X,\mathbb{Q})
and
\beta\in H^{6}(X,\mathbb{Q})
, there exist orientations such that

\frac{\sum_{n}\langle L\rangle_{X,\gamma,\beta,n}^{\mathrm{DT}}q^{n}}{\sum_{n}\langle L\rangle_{X,0,0,n}^{\mathrm{DT}}q^{n}}=\sum_{n}\langle L\rangle_{X,\gamma,\beta,n}^{\mathrm{PT}_{0}}q^{n}.

Remark 3.11
.

In
[
BKP
]
, we argued that for most “interesting”
(2,2)
classes
\gamma\in H^{4}(X,\mathbb{Q})
, one should not work with the Oh-Thomas virtual cycle but rather its
reduced
version. We believe that Conjecture
3.9
holds with invariants defined via the reduced virtual cycle and reduced virtual structure sheaf as well. However, in this paper, the reduced class does not play a significant role for the following reasons:

•

For the virtual Lefschetz principle in Section
4.1
, we consider a smooth connected effective divisor
Y\subset X
and
\gamma
pushed forward from a
(1,1)
-class on
Y
. Then reducing is often unnecessary by
[
BKP
, Prop. 4.37]

•

When
X
is a toric Calabi-Yau 4-fold, we work fully equivariantly with respect to the Calabi-Yau torus
T\cong(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{3}
. The cosections of
\operatorname{Ext}_{X}^{2}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}},I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})_{0}
are typically
T
-equivariant and not
T
-invariant, so the toric theory is non-zero before reducing.

We refer to
[
Sav
]
for reducing of the Borisov-Joyce cycle as an element of
H_{*}(\curly P,\mathbb{Z})
.

3.4.
Variations

Let
X
be a (quasi-projective) Calabi-Yau 4-fold. Fix
v=(0,0,\gamma,\beta,n-\gamma\cdot\mathrm{td}_{2}(X))\in H_{c}^{*}(X,\mathbb{Q})
. Then we have moduli spaces
\curly P:=\curly P_{v}^{(q)}(X)
and a virtual cycle and structure sheaf as discussed in Section
2.2

[\curly P]^{\mathrm{vir}}\in A_{*}(\curly P),\quad\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\curly P}\in K_{0}(\curly P,\mathbb{Z}[\tfrac{1}{2}])

depending on a choice of orientation. Even when
X
is not projective, it may happen that
\curly P
, which is a priori only quasi-projective (Proposition
2.8
), is in fact projective. The following proposition provides many such examples and is proved in essentially the same way as
[
CKM2
, Prop. 3.1]
:

Proposition 3.12
.

\mathrm{(1)}

Let
Y
be a smooth projective Fano 3-fold and
p:X=\mathrm{Tot}(K_{Y})\to Y
the total space of the canonical bundle. Then, for
q\in\{0,1\}
, all elements of
\curly P^{(q)}_{v}(X)
are set theoretically supported on the zero section
Y\subset X
, hence
\curly P^{(q)}_{v}(X)
is projective.

\mathrm{(2)}

Let
S
be a smooth projective surface and
p:X=\mathrm{Tot}(L_{1}\oplus L_{2})\to S
, where
L_{1}^{-1}
,
L_{2}^{-1}
are ample line bundles on
S
satisfying
L_{1}\otimes L_{2}\cong K_{S}
. Then, for
q\in\{0,1\}
, all elements of
\curly P^{(q)}_{v}(X)
are set theoretically supported on the zero section
S\subset X
, hence
\curly P^{(q)}_{v}(X)
is projective.

We assume the following:

Assumption.

\curly P
is projective.

Then the tautological and
K
-theoretic invariants of Definitions
3.1
,
3.3
are well-defined and all results from Sections
3.1
,
3.2
hold. Moreover, we conjecture that the
\mathrm{DT}
–
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
correspondence of Conjecture
3.9
holds in this setting. Finally, Corollary
3.10
also holds in this setting. However, when
X
is quasi-projective, assuming
\curly P
to be projective is a very restrictive assumption. It almost always rules out the case
q=-1
(
\mathrm{DT}
case) due to points “floating to infinity”.

3.4.1.
Equivariant case

Next, we suppose
X
is a (quasi-projective) Calabi-Yau 4-fold with an action by an algebraic torus
T
preserving the Calabi-Yau volume form and admitting a
T
-equivariant good compactification (e.g.
X
is a toric Calabi-Yau 4-fold).
Fix
v=(0,0,\gamma,\beta,n-\gamma\cdot\mathrm{td}_{2}(X))\in H_{c}^{*}(X,\mathbb{Q})
and consider
\curly P:=\curly P_{v}^{(q)}(X)
for
q\in\{-1,0,1\}
. Let
L
be a
T
-equivariant line bundle on
X
.
We
no longer
require
\curly P
to be projective. As discussed in Section
2.2.1
, we then have an equivariant virtual cycle and structure sheaf

[\curly P]^{\mathrm{vir}}\in A_{*}^{T}(\curly P),\quad\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\curly P}\in K_{0}^{T}(\curly P,\mathbb{Z}[\tfrac{1}{2}]).

The action of
T
on
X
lifts to
\curly P
. As we will see in Section
6
, there are many cases where
\curly P
is not projective, but the the fixed locus
\curly P^{T}
is projective. Let us suppose this is the case:

Assumption.

\curly P^{T}
is projective.

As discussed in Section
2.3
, the
K
-theoretic invariants of Definition
3.3
then make sense by the Thomason localization formula

\langle\!\langle L\rangle\!\rangle_{X,v}^{\mathrm{PT}_{q}}\in K_{0}^{T}(\mathrm{pt})_{\mathrm{loc}}(\!(y^{-\frac{1}{2}})\!),

where

K_{0}^{T}(\mathrm{pt})_{\mathrm{loc}}(\!(y^{-\frac{1}{2}})\!)=K_{0}^{T}(\mathrm{pt})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[t,t^{-1}]}\mathbb{Q}(t^{\frac{1}{2}})(\!(y^{-\frac{1}{2}})\!)\cong\mathbb{Q}(t^{\frac{1}{2}})(\!(y^{-\frac{1}{2}})\!)

is the localized equivariant
K
-group of a point,
t
denote the equivariant parameters of
T
, and we added the formal variable
y^{-\frac{1}{2}}
. Similarly, using localization on Chow groups
[
EG
]
, one can define tautological invariants

\langle L\rangle_{X,v}^{\mathrm{PT}_{q}}\in A_{*}^{T}(\mathrm{pt})_{\mathrm{loc}}(\!(m^{-1})\!),

where

A_{0}^{T}(\mathrm{pt})_{\mathrm{loc}}(\!(m^{-1})\!)=A_{0}^{T}(\mathrm{pt})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}[s]}\mathbb{Q}(s)(\!(m^{-1})\!)\cong\mathbb{Q}(s)(\!(m^{-1})\!),

and
s,m
are the equivariant first Chern classes of the equivariant parameters
t,y
respectively.
However, one can no longer use the results of Sections
3.1
and
3.2
, which involved intersection-theoretic arguments on
\curly P
. In the case
X
is toric and
\curly P^{T}
is isolated reduced, we show in a different way that
K
-theoretic invariants specialize to tautological invariants in Section
6.3
.
7
7

7

Actually, we will derive stronger specializations at the level of vertex, edge, and face.

As reviewed in Section
2.3
, fixing an orientation on
\curly P
, one may apply the Oh-Thomas localization formula (Theorem
2.12
).

In Section
6
, we will focus on the setting where
\curly P^{T}
consists of isolated reduced points. Then Proposition
2.13
applies. As indicated in Remark
2.14
, we currently do not have a way to determine the signs
(-1)^{\sigma_{P}}
for any given (global) choice of orientation (except in the case
\curly P=\operatorname{Hilb}^{n}(\mathbb{C}^{4})
studied by the second-named author and Rennemo
[
KR
]
). Therefore, in Proposition
2.13
, we allow
any
choice of signs
(-1)^{\sigma_{P}}
. This means we do
not
require the signs
(-1)^{\sigma_{P}}
to be induced from a global choice of orientation, and allow any sign choice. Put differently: we define equivariant
K
-theoretic invariants by the Oh-Thomas localization formula:

Definition 3.13
.

We define
equivariant
K
-theoretic invariants
by the Oh-Thomas localization formula

\langle\!\langle L\rangle\!\rangle_{X,v}^{\mathrm{PT}_{q}}:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}P\in\curly P^{T}\\
\mathrm{vd}(\{P\})=0\end{subarray}}(-1)^{\sigma_{P}}\frac{\widehat{\Lambda}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}(R\pi_{\curly P*}(\mathbb{F}\otimes L)|_{P}\otimes y^{-1})}{\sqrt{(-1)^{\frac{\mathrm{vd}}{2}}\widehat{\Lambda}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}(T_{\curly P}^{\mathrm{vir}}|_{P}^{\vee})}}\in K_{0}^{T}(\mathrm{pt})_{\mathrm{loc}}(y^{\frac{1}{2}}),

where the combination
(-1)^{\sigma_{P}}\sqrt{(\cdot)|_{P}}
means that for each fixed point
P
we make a choice of square root of
(\cdot)|_{P}
. This definition depends on a choice of square root attached to each fixed point
P
, which is suppressed from the notation of
\langle\!\langle L\rangle\!\rangle_{X,v}^{\mathrm{PT}_{q}}
.

More concretely, this can be rewritten as

\langle\!\langle L\rangle\!\rangle_{X,v}^{\mathrm{PT}_{q}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}=[\mathcal{O}_{X}\to F]\in\curly P^{T}\\
