6.2.
Vertex
\mathrm{DT}
–
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
correspondence

The easiest
\mathrm{DT}
topological vertex is for
\boldsymbol{\mu}
all empty. In this case, the
\mathrm{DT}
topological vertex is a sum over all finite solid partitions viewed as the fixed points of the Hilbert schemes

\bigcup_{n\geq 0}\operatorname{Hilb}^{n}(\mathbb{C}^{4})^{T_{X}}=\bigcup_{n\geq 0}\operatorname{Hilb}^{n}(\mathbb{C}^{4})^{T}.

In
[
KR
]
, it is shown that
\operatorname{Hilb}^{n}(\mathbb{C}^{4})
is
globally
the the zero locus of an isotropic section of an orthogonal bundle on the smooth non-commutative Hilbert scheme
\mathrm{ncHilb}^{n}(\mathbb{C}^{4})
. Moreover, from this description, one can construct an orientation on
\operatorname{Hilb}^{n}(\mathbb{C}^{4})
such that the following holds.

Theorem 6.16
.

[
Nek
,
NP1
,
KR
]

There exist orientations on
\operatorname{Hilb}^{n}(\mathbb{C}^{4})
for which the corresponding signs, induced by the Oh-Thomas localization formula, give

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\langle\!\langle\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}^{4}}\rangle\!\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{4},0,0,n}^{\mathrm{DT}}q^{n}=

\displaystyle\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{DT}}_{\varnothing\varnothing\varnothing\varnothing}(q)=\mathrm{Exp}\Bigg(\frac{[t_{1}t_{2}][t_{1}t_{3}][t_{2}t_{3}][y]}{[t_{1}][t_{2}][t_{3}][t_{4}][y^{\frac{1}{2}}q][y^{\frac{1}{2}}q^{-1}]}\Bigg).

The second equality was conjectured in physics by Nekrasov
[
Nek
]
and conjecturally extended to punctual Quot schemes
30
30

30

In physics parlance: “the coloured case”
of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}^{4}}^{\oplus r}
by Nekrasov-Piazzalunga
[
NP1
]
, who also proposed the formula for the signs (which we discuss in Section
6.4
). We refer to the second equality as the
Magnificent Four formula
. Let us explain the notation on the right hand side. For any formal variable
x
, we set

[x]:=x^{\frac{1}{2}}-x^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

Furthermore, for any formal power series
f(p_{1},\ldots,p_{s};q)
in
\mathbb{Q}(p_{1},\ldots,p_{r})[\![q]\!]
with
f(p_{1},\ldots,p_{s},0)=0
,
its plethystic exponential is defined by

\displaystyle\mathrm{Exp}(f(p_{1},\ldots,p_{s};q))

\displaystyle:=\exp\Big(\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n}f(p_{1}^{n},\ldots,p_{s}^{n};q^{n})\Big).

In the theorem, before taking the plethystic exponential, the Exponent is written as a rational function in
q
and expanded as a formal power series in
q
. For
\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{DT}}_{\varnothing\varnothing\varnothing\varnothing}(q)
, we always fix the signs as in Theorem
6.16
.

We now formulate the main conjecture of this section.

Conjecture 6.17
.

For any plane partitions
\boldsymbol{\mu}=\{\mu_{a}\}_{a=1}^{4}
with no
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
moduli (Definition
5.15
), there exists a choice of
\pm\sqrt{\cdot}
assigned to the
(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{4}
-fixed points such that
31
31

31

Note that the expectation at the end of Remark
6.14
is part of the conjecture: for
\boldsymbol{\mu}
with no
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
moduli and for all
B\in\mathcal{B}_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{(0)}
, the
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
vertex
\mathsf{V}_{I_{B}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}}
has no positive
T
-fixed term.

\frac{\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{DT}}_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}\mu_{4}}(q)}{\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{DT}}_{\varnothing\varnothing\varnothing\varnothing}(q)}=\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{PT}_{0}}_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}\mu_{4}}(q).

For
\boldsymbol{\mu}
all finite (the curve case), the right hand side should be viewed as the
\mathrm{PT}
vertex
[
CKM1
,
Liu
]
. In this case, the above was first conjectured in
[
CKM1
]
. Before we provide computational evidence for this conjecture, we discuss some consequences.

Let
\boldsymbol{\lambda}=\{\lambda_{ab}\}_{1\leq a<b\leq 4}
be finite partitions and
\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}=\{\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{a}\}_{a=1}^{4}
be the minimal plane partitions compatible with
\boldsymbol{\lambda}
(
30
). Then there are no
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
moduli (Corollary
5.19
). Furthermore, by Proposition
2.4
, any
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
pair is a
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
pair with cokernel
Q
of length

\ell(Q)\leq\ell(\curly Ext_{\mathbb{C}^{4}}^{3}(\mathcal{O}_{Z_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}^{4}})).

Therefore

\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{PT}_{0}}_{\mu_{1}^{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mu_{2}^{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mu_{3}^{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mu_{4}^{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}(q)

is a Laurent
polynomial
in
q
with at most
\ell(\curly Ext_{\mathbb{C}^{4}}^{3}(\mathcal{O}_{Z_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{C}^{4}}))+1
terms. Conjecture
6.17
implies that
\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{DT}}_{\mu_{1}^{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mu_{2}^{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mu_{3}^{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mu_{4}^{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}(q)/\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{DT}}_{\varnothing\varnothing\varnothing\varnothing}(q)
is equal to this Laurent polynomial.

Example 6.18
.

In the following examples,
Z_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}
is not Cohen-Macaulay and we used Section
6.1
to determine the
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
topological vertex.

(1)

For
\lambda_{12}=\lambda_{34}=1
, and
\lambda_{ab}=\varnothing
otherwise, we have

\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{PT}_{0}}_{\mu_{1}^{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mu_{2}^{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mu_{3}^{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mu_{4}^{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}(q)=\frac{[t_{1}t_{3}][t_{2}t_{3}]}{[y]}q^{-1}+[t_{1}t_{2}].

(2)

For
\lambda_{12}=1+t_{4}
,
\lambda_{13}=\lambda_{34}=1
, and
\lambda_{ab}=\varnothing
otherwise, we have

\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{PT}_{0}}_{\mu_{1}^{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mu_{2}^{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mu_{3}^{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mu_{4}^{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}(q)=\frac{[t_{1}t_{3}][t_{2}t_{3}][t_{1}t_{2}^{2}t_{3}^{2}]}{[t_{1}t_{2}t_{3}y]}q^{-1}+[t_{1}t_{2}][t_{2}t_{3}].

(3)

For
\lambda_{12}=1+t_{3}
,
\lambda_{34}=1
, and
\lambda_{ab}=\varnothing
otherwise, we have

\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{PT}_{0}}_{\mu_{1}^{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mu_{2}^{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mu_{3}^{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mu_{4}^{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}(q)=\frac{[t_{1}t_{3}][t_{2}t_{3}][t_{1}t_{3}^{2}][t_{2}t_{3}^{2}]}{[y][yt_{3}^{-1}]}q^{-2}+\frac{[t_{1}t_{2}][t_{1}t_{3}][t_{2}t_{3}][t_{3}^{2}]}{[y][t_{3}]}q^{-1}+[t_{1}t_{2}][t_{1}t_{2}t_{3}^{-1}].

The fact that
y
can appear in the denominator is caused by the redistribution. The negative powers in
q
come from the renormalized volumes (Definition
6.2
).

Using the vertex formalism of Section
6.1
, we verified Conjecture
6.17
in several cases. In the following proposition, the “bar” means that we normalized the generating series by its leading term (which is the same for
\mathrm{DT}/\mathrm{PT}_{0}
), so the generating series starts with 1.

Proposition 6.19
.

There exist choices of
\pm\sqrt{\cdot}
such that

\overline{\mathsf{V}}^{\mathrm{DT}}_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}\mu_{4}}(q)=\overline{\mathsf{V}}^{\mathrm{PT}_{0}}_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}\mu_{4}}(q)\,\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{DT}}_{\varnothing\varnothing\varnothing\varnothing}(q)

holds modulo
q^{N}
in the following cases:

\mathrm{(1)}

\lambda_{12}=1
,
\lambda_{ab}=\varnothing
otherwise,
\boldsymbol{\mu}=\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}
, and
N=4
.

\mathrm{(2)}

\lambda_{12}=1+t_{3}
,
\lambda_{ab}=\varnothing
otherwise,
\boldsymbol{\mu}=\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}
, and
N=3
.

\mathrm{(3)}

\lambda_{12}=1+t_{3}+t_{4}
,
\lambda_{ab}=\varnothing
otherwise,
\boldsymbol{\mu}=\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}
, and
N=3
.

\mathrm{(4)}

\lambda_{12}=\lambda_{13}=1
,
\lambda_{ab}=\varnothing
otherwise,
\boldsymbol{\mu}=\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}
, and
N=3
.

\mathrm{(5)}

\lambda_{12}=\lambda_{34}=1
,
\lambda_{ab}=\varnothing
otherwise,
\boldsymbol{\mu}=\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}
, and
N=3
.

\mathrm{(6)}

\lambda_{12}=\lambda_{13}=\lambda_{23}=1
,
\lambda_{ab}=\varnothing
otherwise,
\boldsymbol{\mu}=\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}
, and
N=3
.

\mathrm{(7)}

\lambda_{ab}=1
for all
a<b
,
\boldsymbol{\mu}=\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}
, and
N=3
.

\mathrm{(8)}

\lambda_{12}=\lambda_{34}=\lambda_{23}=1
,
\lambda_{ab}=\varnothing
otherwise,
\boldsymbol{\mu}=\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}
, and
N=3
.

\mathrm{(9)}

\lambda_{12}=1+t_{4}
,
\lambda_{13}=\lambda_{34}=1,\lambda_{ab}=\varnothing
otherwise,
\boldsymbol{\mu}=\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}
, and
N=3
.

\mathrm{(10)}

\lambda_{12}=1+t_{3}
,
\lambda_{34}=1,\lambda_{ab}=\varnothing
otherwise,
\boldsymbol{\mu}=\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}
, and
N=3
.

\mathrm{(11)}

\mu_{1}=\frac{1}{1-t_{2}}+t_{3}
,
\mu_{2}=\frac{1}{1-t_{1}}
,
\mu_{a}=\varnothing
otherwise, and
N=4
.

\mathrm{(12)}

\mu_{1}=\frac{1}{1-t_{2}}+t_{3}+t_{2}t_{3}
,
\mu_{2}=\frac{1}{1-t_{1}}
,
\mu_{a}=\varnothing
otherwise, and
N=3
.

\mathrm{(13)}

\mu_{1}=\frac{1}{1-t_{2}}+t_{3}+t_{3}^{2}
,
\mu_{2}=\frac{1}{1-t_{1}}
,
\mu_{a}=\varnothing
otherwise, and
N=3
.

\mathrm{(14)}

\mu_{1}=\frac{1}{1-t_{2}}
,
\mu_{2}=\frac{1}{1-t_{1}}
,
\mu_{3}=1
,
\mu_{4}=\varnothing
, and
N=3
.

\mathrm{(15)}

\mu_{1}=\frac{1}{1-t_{2}}+t_{3}
,
\mu_{2}=\frac{1}{1-t_{1}}+t_{4}
,
\mu_{3}=1
,
\mu_{4}=\varnothing
, and
N=2
.

In this list, the number of fixed points on the
\mathrm{DT}
side can be rather large. In case (1), for 3 embedded points we have 19 torus fixed points. In case (11), for 3 embedded points we have 33 torus fixed points.

Since face and edge terms coincide for
\mathrm{DT}
and
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
theory, Proposition
6.10
, Remark
6.14
, and Conjecture
6.17
imply the
global

T
-equivariant
K
-theoretic
\mathrm{DT}
–
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
correspondence (Conjecture
3.14
):

Corollary 6.20
.

Let
X
be a toric Calabi-Yau 4-fold and let
L
be a
T_{X}
-equivariant line bundle on
X
. Let
\gamma\in H_{c}^{4}(X,\mathbb{Q})
and
\beta\in H_{c}^{6}(X,\mathbb{Q})
such that
\curly P_{v}^{(0)}(X)^{T_{X}}=\curly P_{v}^{(0)}(X)^{T}
is empty or 0-dimensional and reduced for
v=(0,0,\gamma,\beta,n-\gamma\cdot\mathrm{td}_{2}(X))
and all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
. Suppose Conjecture
6.17
holds. Then there are choices of
\pm\sqrt{\cdot}
such that

\frac{\sum_{n}\langle\!\langle L\rangle\!\rangle_{X,\gamma,\beta,n}^{\mathrm{DT}}q^{n}}{\sum_{n}\langle\!\langle L\rangle\!\rangle_{X,0,0,n}^{\mathrm{DT}}q^{n}}=\sum_{n}\langle\!\langle L\rangle\!\rangle_{X,\gamma,\beta,n}^{\mathrm{PT}_{0}}q^{n}.

6.3.
Cohomological limits

Let
X
be a toric Calabi-Yau 4-fold and
L
a
T_{X}
-equivariant line bundle on
X
. Let
\curly P:=\curly P_{v}^{(q)}(X)
for any
v
as in (
33
) and
q\in\{-1,0,1\}
. Besides the
K
-theoretic invariants
\langle\!\langle L\rangle\!\rangle_{X,v}^{\mathrm{PT}_{q}}
,
we are also interested in the following “tautological” and “cohomological” invariants

\displaystyle\langle L\rangle_{X,v}^{\mathrm{PT}_{q}}\in\frac{\mathbb{Q}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3},s_{4},m)}{(s_{1}+s_{2}+s_{3}+s_{4})},\quad\int_{[\curly P]^{\mathrm{vir}}}1\in\frac{\mathbb{Q}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3},s_{4})}{(s_{1}+s_{2}+s_{3}+s_{4})},

where the former were defined in Section
3.4
, and we recall that
c_{1}^{T}(t_{i})=s_{i}
and
c_{1}^{\mathbb{C}^{*}}(y)=m
are the cohomological equivariant parameters.
Similar to the
K
-theoretic case, these invariants can be calculated using the vertex, edge, face terms
\widetilde{\mathsf{V}}_{\alpha}
,
\widetilde{\mathsf{E}}_{\alpha\beta}
,
\widetilde{\mathsf{F}}_{f}
of Section
6.1
. The only differences are the following:

•

tautological invariants: replace

[t_{1}^{i_{1}}t_{2}^{i_{2}}t_{3}^{i_{3}}t_{4}^{i_{4}}y^{i_{5}}]:=t_{1}^{\frac{i_{1}}{2}}t_{2}^{\frac{i_{2}}{2}}t_{3}^{\frac{i_{3}}{2}}t_{4}^{\frac{i_{4}}{2}}y^{\frac{i_{5}}{2}}-t_{1}^{-\frac{i_{1}}{2}}t_{2}^{-\frac{i_{2}}{2}}t_{3}^{-\frac{i_{3}}{2}}t_{4}^{-\frac{i_{4}}{2}}y^{-\frac{i_{5}}{2}}

by

e(t_{1}^{i_{1}}t_{2}^{i_{2}}t_{3}^{i_{3}}t_{4}^{i_{4}}y^{i_{5}}):=i_{1}s_{1}+i_{2}s_{2}+i_{3}s_{3}+i_{4}s_{4}+i_{5}m,

•

cohomological invariants: the same as the previous case but omit all terms involving
y
from the vertex, edge, and face terms of Section
6.1
, in other words, use
\mathsf{V}_{\alpha}
,
\mathsf{E}_{\alpha\beta}
,
\mathsf{F}_{f}
instead of
\widetilde{\mathsf{V}}_{\alpha}
,
\widetilde{\mathsf{E}}_{\alpha\beta}
,
\widetilde{\mathsf{F}}_{f}
.

For the vertex formalism in the curve case we have
[
CKM1
, Prop. 2.3]

\operatorname{rk}(\widetilde{\mathsf{V}}_{\alpha})=\operatorname{rk}(\widetilde{\mathsf{E}}_{\alpha\beta})=0.

This is no longer the case for surfaces. Then
\widetilde{\mathsf{V}}_{\alpha}
,
\widetilde{\mathsf{E}}_{\alpha\beta}
,
\widetilde{\mathsf{F}}_{f}
can have non-zero rank (e.g. see Example
6.18
).
However, we have the following weaker result, which we formulate for the case
q=-1
, but it also holds for
q=0,1
.

Lemma 6.21
.

Let
Z,Z^{\prime}\subset\mathbb{C}^{4}
be 2-dimensional
(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{4}
-invariant closed subschemes with the same underlying pure 2-dimensional sheaf, i.e.,
Z_{1}=Z_{1}^{\prime}
. Then

\operatorname{rk}(\widetilde{\mathsf{V}}_{Z})=\operatorname{rk}(\widetilde{\mathsf{V}}_{Z^{\prime}}).

Proof.

Consider the definition of
\widetilde{\mathsf{V}}_{Z}
(Definition
6.3
) and make the substitutions of Definition
6.2
(where we write
Z=Z_{\alpha}
,
W=W_{\alpha}
,
W_{i}:=W_{\alpha\beta_{i}}
,
W_{ij}=Z_{\alpha\beta_{i}\beta_{j}}
). Taking
t_{1}=\cdots=t_{4}=1
, an explicit calculation gives

(44)

\widetilde{\mathsf{V}}_{Z}\Big|_{t_{1}=\cdots=t_{4}=1}=\Big(W+\overline{W}-y^{-1}W-y\overline{W}-\sum_{i,j,k,l\in\{1,2,3,4\}\atop\textrm{distinct }i<j,k<l}W_{ij}\overline{W}_{kl}\Big)\Big|_{t_{1}=\cdots=t_{4}=1},

which is independent of
W_{i}
. We see that for fixed 2-dimensional asymptotics
W_{ij}
, adding or removing embedded points or curves keeps the rank of
\widetilde{\mathsf{V}}_{Z}
unchanged as claimed.
∎

We formulate the following result for
q=-1
, but it holds analogously for
q=0,1
.

Proposition 6.22
.

Let
Z,Z^{\prime}\subset\mathbb{C}^{4}
be 2-dimensional
(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{4}
-invariant closed subschemes with the same underlying pure 2-dimensional sheaf, i.e.,
Z_{1}=Z_{1}^{\prime}
. Suppose
\widetilde{V}_{Z^{\prime}}
has no
T
-fixed term. Then

\displaystyle\lim_{b\mapsto 0}\frac{[-\widetilde{\mathsf{V}}_{Z}]}{[-\widetilde{\mathsf{V}}_{Z^{\prime}}]}\Big|_{t_{i}=e^{bs_{i}},y=e^{bm}}

\displaystyle=\frac{e(-\widetilde{\mathsf{V}}_{Z})}{e(-\widetilde{\mathsf{V}}_{Z^{\prime}})},

\displaystyle\lim_{b\mapsto 0\atop m\to\infty}\frac{[-\widetilde{\mathsf{V}}_{Z}]/(-m^{2})^{|Z|}}{[-\widetilde{\mathsf{V}}_{Z^{\prime}}]/(-m^{2})^{|Z^{\prime}|}}\Big|_{t_{i}=e^{bs_{i}},y=e^{bm}}

\displaystyle=\frac{e(-\mathsf{V}_{Z})}{e(-\mathsf{V}_{Z^{\prime}})},

where
|Z|
,
|Z^{\prime}|
denote the renormalized volumes of
Z,Z^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Using multi-index notation
\eta=(t_{1},t_{2},t_{3},t_{4},y)
, for any
rank 0
finite complex
E=\sum_{v}\eta^{v}-\sum_{w}\eta^{w}
, we have

[E]=\frac{\prod_{v}[\tau^{v}]}{\prod_{w}[\tau^{w}]}\Big|_{t_{i}=e^{bs_{i}},y=e^{bm}}=\frac{\prod_{v}(v_{1}s_{1}+v_{2}s_{2}+v_{3}s_{3}+v_{4}s_{4}+v_{5}m+O(b))}{\prod_{w}(w_{1}s_{1}+w_{2}s_{2}+w_{3}s_{3}+w_{4}s_{4}+w_{5}m+O(b))},

and therefore

\lim_{b\mapsto 0}\widehat{\Lambda}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}E^{*}=\lim_{b\mapsto 0}[E]=e(E).

The first statement then follows from Lemma
6.21
.

Now we turn to the second statement. Let
Z\subset\mathbb{C}^{4}
be the 2-dimensional subscheme corresponding to
\pi
and use the notation of Definition
6.2
(with
Z=Z_{\alpha}
,
W=W_{\alpha}
). The only term involving
y
in
[-\widetilde{\mathsf{V}}_{Z}]/(-m^{2})^{|Z|}
is
[y^{-1}W+y\overline{W}]/(-m^{2})^{|Z|}
, where
|Z|=\operatorname{rk}(W)
is the renormalized volume (Definition
6.2
). We use multi-index notation
\tau=(t_{1},t_{2},t_{3},t_{4})
. Writing
W=\sum_{v}\tau^{v}-\sum_{w}\tau^{w}
and taking the limit
b\to 0
, it contributes

\displaystyle\frac{[y^{-1}W+y\overline{W}]}{(-m^{2})^{|Z|}}

\displaystyle=(-m^{2})^{-(\sum_{v}1-\sum_{w}1)}\frac{\prod_{v}(-1)(v_{1}s_{1}+v_{2}s_{2}+v_{3}s_{3}+v_{4}s_{4}-m)^{2}}{\prod_{w}(-1)(w_{1}s_{1}+w_{2}s_{2}+w_{3}s_{3}+w_{4}s_{4}-m)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\prod_{v}(\frac{v_{1}}{m}s_{1}+\frac{v_{2}}{m}s_{2}+\frac{v_{3}}{m}s_{3}+\frac{v_{4}}{m}s_{4}-1)^{2}}{\prod_{w}(\frac{w_{1}}{m}s_{1}+\frac{w_{2}}{m}s_{2}+\frac{w_{3}}{m}s_{3}+\frac{w_{4}}{m}s_{4}-1)^{2}}.

This term goes to 1 as
m\to\infty
.
∎

For
q=-1,0
, we accordingly define the tautological and cohomological topological vertex

\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{PT}_{q},\textrm{taut}}_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}\mu_{4}}(q),\quad\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{PT}_{q},\textrm{coho}}_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}\mu_{4}}(q).

In the following corollary, the “bar” means that we normalized the generating series by its leading term, so the generating series starts with 1.

Corollary 6.23
.

Let
{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
be plane partitions corresponding to subschemes of dimension
\leq 1
of
\mathbb{C}^{3}
. Then for
q=-1,0
we have

\displaystyle\lim_{b\mapsto 0}\overline{\mathsf{V}}^{\mathrm{PT}_{q},\mathrm{taut}}_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}\mu_{4}}(q)\Big|_{t_{i}=e^{bs_{i}},y=e^{bm}}

\displaystyle=\overline{\mathsf{V}}^{\mathrm{PT}_{q}}_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}\mu_{4}}(q),

\displaystyle\lim_{b\mapsto 0\atop m\to\infty}\overline{\mathsf{V}}^{\mathrm{PT}_{q},\mathrm{coho}}_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}\mu_{4}}(q^{\prime})\Big|_{t_{i}=e^{bs_{i}},y=e^{bm},q^{\prime}=-\frac{q}{m}}

\displaystyle=\overline{\mathsf{V}}^{\mathrm{PT}_{q}}_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}\mu_{4}}(q).

In particular, the
K
-theoretic vertex
\mathrm{DT}
–
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
correspondence (Conjecture
6.17
) implies a tautological and cohomological vertex
\mathrm{DT}
–
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
correspondence (for the same choice of signs). Consequently, we also obtain global analogs of Corollary
6.20
for tautological and numerical insertions.

6.4.
Local 3-folds

For
Y
a smooth quasi-projective toric 3-fold, we consider the toric Calabi-Yau 4-fold

p:X=\mathrm{Tot}(K_{Y})\to Y,

i.e., the total space of the canonical line bundle on
Y
. Denote by
T_{Y}
the torus acting on
Y
and by
\mathbb{C}^{*}
the torus acting on the fibres, then
T_{X}=T_{Y}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}
. The torus
T\leq T_{X}
preserving the Calabi-Yau volume form is isomorphic to
T_{Y}
. Let
v=(0,0,\gamma,\beta,n-\gamma\cdot\mathrm{td}_{2}(X))\in H_{c}^{*}(X,\mathbb{Q})
and
\curly P:=\curly P_{v}^{(q)}(X)
. In Section
4.2
, assuming
\curly P^{T}
is 0-dimensional and reduced, we deduced

(45)

\langle\!\langle L\rangle\!\rangle_{X,v}^{\mathrm{PT}_{q}}=\sum_{I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}=[\mathcal{O}_{X}\to F]\in\curly P^{T}}(-1)^{\sigma_{I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}}}\frac{\widehat{\Lambda}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}(R\Gamma(X,F\otimes L)\otimes y^{-1})}{\widehat{\Lambda}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}(R\Gamma(Y,p_{*}F)-R\operatorname{Hom}_{Y}(p_{*}F,p_{*}F))^{\vee}},

for any
T_{X}
-equivariant line bundle
L
on
X
and choice of sign attached to each
T
-fixed point. Recall that in Section
4.2
, we focused on the case
L=\mathcal{O}_{X}(Y)
, but now
L
is arbitrary.

Let
I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}=[\mathcal{O}_{X}\to F]\in\curly P^{T_{X}}
. In this section, we develop a vertex formalism for the calculation of

R\Gamma(Y,p_{*}F)-R\operatorname{Hom}_{Y}(p_{*}F,p_{*}F))

and comment on the signs
(-1)^{\sigma_{I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}}}
.

We start with the following definitions. Note that for
X=\mathrm{Tot}(K_{Y})
, we have
V(X)=V(Y)
,
E(X)=E(Y)
,
F(X)=F(Y)
, because we only consider compact edges and faces. For notational simplicity, we consider the case
q=-1
, but the cases
q=0,1
hold similarly (subject to the modifications of Remarks
6.14
and
6.15
). Let
Z\subset X
be a closed subscheme of dimension
\leq 2
with proper support. For
\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{N}\in V(X)
, such that
U_{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{N}}\cong(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{k}\times\mathbb{C}^{4-k}
with
k=0,1,2
, we always label coordinates on
(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{k}\times\mathbb{C}^{4-k}
in such a way that
Z\cap U_{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{N}}
is set theoretically (but not necessarily scheme theoretically!) supported on
Z(x_{4})
. We also choose the coordinates such that
T_{X}
acts on
U_{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{N}}
by the standard torus action (
35
). In other words, locally, the fibre coordinate of
X=\mathrm{Tot}(K_{Y})
always corresponds to
x_{4}
. We use the notation of Section
6.1
.

Definition 6.24
.

We define the
halved vertex term

\displaystyle\mathsf{v}_{\alpha}

\displaystyle:=Z_{\alpha}+\frac{P_{123}}{t_{1}t_{2}t_{3}}Z_{\alpha}\overline{Z}_{\alpha}+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\frac{\mathsf{a}_{\alpha\beta_{i}}}{1-t_{i}}+\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq 3}\frac{\mathsf{a}_{\alpha\beta_{i}\beta_{j}}}{(1-t_{i})(1-t_{j})},

where, for any distinct
i,j,k\in\{1,2,3\}
, we set

\displaystyle\mathsf{a}_{\alpha\beta_{i}}

\displaystyle:=-Z_{\alpha\beta_{i}}+\frac{P_{jk}}{t_{j}t_{k}}Z_{\alpha\beta_{i}}\overline{Z}_{\alpha\beta_{i}},

\displaystyle\mathsf{a}_{\alpha\beta_{i}\beta_{j}}

\displaystyle:=Z_{\alpha\beta_{i}\beta_{j}}+\frac{P_{k}}{t_{k}}Z_{\alpha\beta_{i}\beta_{j}}\overline{Z}_{\alpha\beta_{i}\beta_{j}}.

When we want to stress the dependence of these expressions on
Z_{\alpha},Z_{\alpha\beta},Z_{\alpha\beta\gamma}
, we write
\mathsf{v}_{Z_{\alpha}},\mathsf{a}_{Z_{\alpha\beta}},\mathsf{a}_{Z_{\alpha\beta\gamma}}
.

Definition 6.25
.

For any distinct
i,j,k\in\{1,2,3\}
, we define the
halved edge term
by

\displaystyle\mathsf{e}_{\alpha\beta_{i}}

\displaystyle:=\frac{t_{i}^{-1}\mathsf{b}_{\alpha\beta_{i}}(t_{j},t_{k},t_{4})}{1-t_{i}^{-1}}-\frac{\mathsf{b}_{\alpha\beta_{i}}(t_{i}^{-m_{\alpha\beta_{i}}}t_{j},t_{i}^{-m_{\alpha\beta_{i}}^{\prime}}t_{k},t_{i}^{-m_{\alpha\beta_{i}}^{\prime\prime}}t_{4})}{1-t_{i}^{-1}},

where

\displaystyle\mathsf{b}_{\alpha\beta_{i}}

\displaystyle:=\mathsf{a}_{\alpha\beta_{i}}+\sum_{a\in\{i,j,k\}\setminus\{i\}}\frac{\mathsf{a}_{\alpha\beta_{i}\beta_{a}}}{1-t_{a}}.

When we want to indicate the dependence of these expressions on
Z_{\alpha},Z_{\alpha\beta}
, we write
\mathsf{e}_{Z_{\alpha\beta}},\mathsf{b}_{Z_{\alpha\beta}}
.

Definition 6.26
.

Fix
f\in F(X)=F(Y)
. Denote by
Z_{f}\subset X
the unique
T_{X}
-fixed 2-dimensional closed subscheme set-theoretically supported on
S_{f}\subset Y\subset X
such that

Z_{f}\cap U_{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\alpha_{3}}=Z\cap U_{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\alpha_{3}}

for any distinct
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3}\in f
. We define the
halved face term
by

\mathsf{f}_{f}:=\operatorname{tr}_{R\Gamma(Y,p_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Z_{f}})-R\operatorname{Hom}_{Y}(p_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Z_{f}},p_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Z_{f}})}.

When we want to stress the dependence of
\mathsf{f}_{f}
on
Z_{f}
we write
\mathsf{f}_{Z_{f}}
.

For
\dim Z=0
,
\mathsf{v}_{\alpha}
reduces to the halved vertex term first introduced by Nekrasov-Piazzalunga
[
Nek
,
NP1
]
. For
\dim Z=1
,
\mathsf{v}_{\alpha}
and
\mathsf{e}_{\alpha\beta}
reduce to the halved vertex and edge terms studied in
[
CKM1
]
. The following lemma justifies the use of the adjective “halved”.

Lemma 6.27
.

For all
\alpha\in V(X)
,
\alpha\beta\in E(X)
,
f\in F(X)
, the following equations hold modulo the relation
t_{1}t_{2}t_{3}t_{4}=1

\displaystyle\mathsf{v}_{\alpha}+\overline{\mathsf{v}}_{\alpha}=\mathsf{V}_{\alpha},\quad\mathsf{e}_{\alpha\beta}+\overline{\mathsf{e}}_{\alpha\beta}=\mathsf{E}_{\alpha\beta},\quad\mathsf{f}_{f}+\overline{\mathsf{f}}_{f}=\mathsf{F}_{f}.

Proof.

The first two equations follow from a straight-forward calculation involving identities such as

\frac{P_{123}}{t_{1}t_{2}t_{3}}+\frac{\overline{P}_{123}}{t_{1}^{-1}t_{2}^{-1}t_{3}^{-1}}=-\frac{P_{1234}}{t_{1}t_{2}t_{3}t_{4}},

which uses the relation
t_{1}t_{2}t_{3}t_{4}=1
. The third identity follows from (
18
).
∎

The following proposition is proved similarly to Theorem
6.6
.

Proposition 6.28
.

Consider
p:X=\mathrm{Tot}(K_{Y})\to Y
, where
Y
is a smooth quasi-projective toric 3-fold. Then for any
T_{X}
-fixed closed subscheme
Z\subset X
with proper support and dimension
\leq 2
, we have

R\Gamma(Y,p_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Z})-R\operatorname{Hom}_{Y}(p_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Z},p_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Z})=\sum_{\alpha\in V(X)}\mathsf{v}_{\alpha}+\sum_{\alpha\beta\in E(X)}\mathsf{e}_{\alpha\beta}+\sum_{f\in F(X)}\mathsf{f}_{f},

where
\mathsf{v}_{\alpha},\mathsf{e}_{\alpha\beta},\mathsf{f}_{f}
are Laurent polynomials.

Proof.

Denote by
\{U_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in V(Y)}
the open cover of
Y
by maximal
T_{Y}
-invariant affine open subsets. Choose a total order on the indices
\alpha\in V(Y)
. Using Čech cohomology and the local-to-global spectral sequence as in the beginning of Section
6.1
, we obtain

\displaystyle R\Gamma(Y,p_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Z})-R\operatorname{Hom}_{Y}(p_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Z},p_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Z})=

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\sum_{\alpha\in V(Y)}\Big(R\Gamma(U_{\alpha},p_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Z})-R\operatorname{Hom}_{U_{\alpha}}(p_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Z},p_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Z})\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad-\sum_{\alpha<\beta\in V(Y)}\Big(R\Gamma(U_{\alpha\beta},p_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Z})-R\operatorname{Hom}_{U_{\alpha\beta}}(p_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Z},p_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Z})\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad+\sum_{\alpha<\beta<\gamma\in V(Y)}\Big(R\Gamma(U_{\alpha\beta\gamma},p_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Z})-R\operatorname{Hom}_{U_{\alpha\beta\gamma}}(p_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Z},p_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Z})\Big)+\cdots,

where we suppress restrictions to
U_{\alpha}
,
U_{\alpha\beta}
,
U_{\alpha\beta\gamma}
,
\ldots

There exist finitely many
T_{Y}
-invariant closed subschemes

Y\supset Z(0)\supset Z(1)\supset\cdots

such that

p_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Z}=\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}\mathcal{O}_{Z(i)}\otimes t_{4}^{i}.

We denote their restrictions by
Z_{\alpha}(i)=Z(i)\cap U_{\alpha}
,
Z_{\alpha\beta}(i)=Z(i)\cap U_{\alpha\beta}
,
Z_{\alpha\beta\gamma}(i)=Z(i)\cap U_{\alpha\beta\gamma}
, etc. Using Taylor resolutions
[
Tay
]
, it is easy to see that

\displaystyle R\Gamma(U_{\alpha},p_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Z})-R\operatorname{Hom}_{U_{\alpha}}(p_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Z},p_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Z})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\geq 0}R\Gamma(U_{\alpha},\mathcal{O}_{Z_{\alpha}(i)})t_{4}^{i}-\sum_{i,j\geq 0}R\operatorname{Hom}_{U_{\alpha}}(\mathcal{O}_{Z_{\alpha}(i)},\mathcal{O}_{Z_{\alpha}(j)})t_{4}^{j-i}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\geq 0}Z_{\alpha}(i)t_{4}^{i}-\sum_{i,j\geq 0}\frac{P_{123}}{t_{1}t_{2}t_{3}}\overline{Z_{\alpha}(i)}Z_{\alpha}(j)t_{4}^{j-i}=Z_{\alpha}+\frac{P_{123}}{t_{1}t_{2}t_{3}}Z_{\alpha}\overline{Z}_{\alpha}.

Let
\alpha\beta\in E(Y)
. Let
\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{N}\in V(Y)
such that
\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{N}\in f
for some
f\in F(Y)
, where for
N=2
we assume
\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}
is not an edge. Then
U_{\alpha\beta}\cong\mathbb{C}^{*}\times\mathbb{C}^{2}
and
U_{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{N}}\cong(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{2}\times\mathbb{C}
, and we similarly obtain

\displaystyle R\Gamma(U_{\alpha\beta},p_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Z})-R\operatorname{Hom}_{U_{\alpha\beta}}(p_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Z},p_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Z})=\delta(t_{1})\Big(Z_{\alpha\beta}-\frac{P_{23}}{t_{2}t_{3}}Z_{\alpha\beta}\overline{Z}_{\alpha\beta}\Big),

\displaystyle R\Gamma(U_{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{N}},p_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Z})-R\operatorname{Hom}_{U_{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{N}}}(p_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Z},p_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Z})=

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\delta(t_{1})\delta(t_{2})\Big(Z_{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{N}}+\frac{P_{3}}{t_{3}}Z_{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{N}}\overline{Z}_{\alpha_{1}\cdots\alpha_{N}}\Big).

For the remainder of the proof, we can follow the proof of Theorem
6.6
.
∎

Remark 6.29
.

Similar to Remark
6.7
, we can obtain a nice expression for
\mathsf{f}_{f}
and
f\in F(Y)
. For a 2-dimensional
T_{X}
-fixed closed subscheme
Z\subset X
with proper support, denote by
\lambda_{f}
the finite partition corresponding to the subscheme
Z_{f}
from Definition
6.5
. Let

\mathsf{a}_{f}(t_{3},t_{4}):=Z_{\lambda_{f}}+\frac{P_{3}}{t_{3}}Z_{\lambda_{f}}\overline{Z}_{\lambda_{f}}\in\mathbb{Z}[t_{3}^{\pm 1},t_{4}^{\pm 1}],

where
Z_{\lambda_{f}}=\sum_{(i,j)\in\lambda_{f}}t_{3}^{i}t_{4}^{j}
. Then

(46)

\mathsf{f}_{f}=\sum_{i=1}^{e(S_{f})}\frac{\mathsf{a}_{f}(t_{3}^{(i)},t_{4}^{(i)})}{(1-t_{1}^{(i)})(1-t_{2}^{(i)})},

where the right hand side is an element of
\mathbb{Q}[t_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,t_{4}^{\pm 1}]\subset\mathbb{Q}(t_{1},\ldots,t_{4})
.
We observe that, modulo the relation
t_{1}t_{2}t_{3}t_{4}=1
, it is also clear by direct computation that the sum of (
46
) and its conjugate equals (
42
). In fact, (
46
) provides a “half” in
K
-theory of (
42
) for any toric Calabi-Yau 4-fold (not necessarily of the form
X=\mathrm{Tot}(K_{Y})
).

Next, we incorporate the tautological insertion

\widehat{\Lambda}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}(R\pi_{\curly P*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{Z}}\otimes y^{-1})

into the vertex formalism, which we already carried out in Section
6.1
. We define for all
i,j,k\in\{1,2,3\}
distinct

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathsf{v}}_{\alpha}:=

\displaystyle\mathsf{v}_{\alpha}-y\overline{Z}_{\alpha}+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\frac{y\overline{Z}_{\alpha\beta_{i}}}{1-t_{i}^{-1}}-\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq 3}\frac{y\overline{Z}_{\alpha\beta_{i}\beta_{j}}}{(1-t_{i}^{-1})(1-t_{j}^{-1})},

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathsf{e}}_{\alpha\beta_{i}}:=

\displaystyle\mathsf{e}_{\alpha\beta_{i}}+\frac{t_{i}}{1-t_{i}}\Bigg\{y\overline{Z}_{\alpha\beta_{i}}-\sum_{a\in\{j,k\}}\frac{y\overline{Z}_{\alpha\beta_{i}\beta_{a}}}{1-t_{a}^{-1}}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{1}{1-t_{i}}\Bigg\{y\overline{Z}_{\alpha\beta_{i}}-\sum_{a\in\{j,k\}}\frac{y\overline{Z}_{\alpha\beta_{i}\beta_{a}}}{1-t_{a}^{-1}}\Bigg\}\Bigg|_{(t_{j}t_{i}^{-m_{\alpha\beta_{i}}},t_{k}t_{i}^{-m_{\alpha\beta_{i}}^{\prime}},t_{4}t_{i}^{-m_{\alpha\beta_{i}}^{\prime\prime}})},

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathsf{f}}_{f}:=

\displaystyle\mathsf{f}_{f}-yR\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{Z_{f}})^{\vee}.

Combining (
45
) and Proposition
6.28
, we obtain the following result.

Proposition 6.30
.

Let
X=\mathrm{Tot}(K_{Y})
for a smooth quasi-projective toric 3-fold
Y
and fix
v=(0,0,\gamma,\beta,n-\gamma\cdot\mathrm{td}_{2}(X))\in H_{c}^{*}(X,\mathbb{Q})
. Let
\curly P^{T}:=\curly P_{v}^{(-1)}(X)^{T}
. Then

\langle\!\langle\mathcal{O}_{X}\rangle\!\rangle_{X,v}^{\mathrm{DT}}=\sum_{Z\in\curly P^{T}}\pm\Big[-\sum_{\alpha\in V(Y)}\widetilde{\mathsf{v}}_{Z_{\alpha}}-\sum_{\alpha\beta\in E(Y)}\widetilde{\mathsf{e}}_{Z_{\alpha\beta}}-\sum_{f\in F(Y)}\widetilde{\mathsf{f}}_{Z_{f}}\Big],

where the right hand side depends on a choice of
\pm
for each fixed point.

By Lemmas
6.12
and
6.27
,
\mathsf{v}_{\alpha}
,
\mathsf{e}_{\alpha\beta}
,
\mathsf{f}_{f}
have no positive
T
-fixed part, so we may also write

\displaystyle\langle\!\langle\mathcal{O}_{X}\rangle\!\rangle_{X,v}^{\mathrm{DT}}=\sum_{Z\in\curly P^{T}}\pm\prod_{\alpha\in V(Y)}[-\widetilde{\mathsf{v}}_{Z_{\alpha}}]\cdot\prod_{\alpha\beta\in E(Y)}[-\widetilde{\mathsf{e}}_{Z_{\alpha\beta}}]\cdot\prod_{f\in F(Y)}[-\widetilde{\mathsf{f}}_{Z_{f}}].

We stress that the elements of
\curly P^{T}
are set theoretically, but not necessarily scheme theoretically, supported on the zero section
Y\subset X
. The invariants
\langle\!\langle L\rangle\!\rangle_{X,v}^{\mathrm{DT}}
, for other
T_{X}
-equivariant line bundles
L
on
X
, can also easily be incorporated into the vertex formalism by Remark
6.11
.

Let
\boldsymbol{\mu}=\{\mu_{a}\}_{a=1}^{4}
be a collection of plane partitions corresponding to subschemes of dimension
\leq 1
of
\mathbb{C}^{3}
such that
Z_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
(Definition
5.5
) is
set theoretically
supported on
Z(x_{4})
. In particular
\mu_{4}=\varnothing
. Then the
\mathrm{DT}
topological vertex equals (Definition
6.13
)

(47)

\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{DT}}_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}\varnothing}(q)=\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{P}_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}\pm[-\widetilde{\mathsf{v}}_{\pi}]\,q^{|\pi|},

where
\pm
indicates that this expression involves a choice of sign for each
\pi\in\mathcal{P}_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
.

Remark 6.31
.

Just like in Remark
6.14
and
6.15
, the results of this section so far hold similarly for
q=0,1
.

In the remainder of this section, we take
q=-1
and comment on the signs in (
47
). It is tempting to think that the “natural” sign of every solid partition in (
47
) is
+1
. However, neither the Magnificent Four formula (Theorem
6.16
) nor the
\mathrm{DT}
–
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
vertex correspondence (Conjecture
6.17
) hold for this choice of signs.

Suppose
\boldsymbol{\mu}
are all empty. Then it was first found in physics by Nekrasov-Piazzalunga
[
Nek
,
NP1
]
and proved mathematically by the second-named author and Rennemo
[
KR
]
, that the Magnificent Four formula holds when we assign the sign

(48)

(-1)^{|\pi|+|\{(i,i,i,j)\in\pi\,:\,i<j\}|}

to each finite solid partition
\pi
. Therefore, (
48
) provides the correct sign choice when all
\boldsymbol{\mu}
are empty.

Suppose
\boldsymbol{\mu}
are
finite
plane partitions and
\mu_{4}=\varnothing
. For all verifications of the
\mathrm{DT}
–
\mathrm{PT}
(curve) vertex correspondence performed in
[
CKM1
]
, it was found that, on the
\mathrm{DT}
side, the following choice of sign for each
\pi\in\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}}
works

(49)

(-1)^{|\pi|}\prod_{w=(i,i,i,j)\in\pi\atop i<j}(-1)^{1-|\{\textrm{legs containing }w\}|}.

When
\boldsymbol{\mu}
are all empty, (
49
) reduces to (
48
). Conjecturally, on the
\mathrm{DT}
side, this provides the sign choice for which the
\mathrm{DT}
–
\mathrm{PT}
(curve) vertex correspondence holds when all
\boldsymbol{\mu}
are finite plane partitions and
\mu_{4}=\varnothing
.

Now let
\boldsymbol{\mu}=\{\mu_{a}\}_{a=1}^{4}
be a collection of plane partitions corresponding to subschemes of dimension
\leq 1
of
\mathbb{C}^{3}
such that
Z_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
is set theoretically supported on
Z(x_{4})
(in particular
\mu_{4}=\varnothing
). Let
\pi\in\mathcal{P}_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
and denote the corresponding subscheme by
Z_{\pi}\subset\mathbb{C}^{4}
. Denote by
W:=W_{\alpha}
the Laurent polynomial in Definition
6.2
(it is in fact a polynomial since
q=-1
). Then
|\pi|=\operatorname{rk}(W)
is the renormalized volume. We associate to
\pi
the sign

(50)

(-1)^{|\pi|+\mu_{\pi}},\quad\mu_{\pi}:=\sum_{(i,i,i,j)\in\pi\atop i<j}\frac{1}{(i!)^{3}j!}\frac{\partial^{3i+j}W}{\partial t_{1}^{i}\partial t_{2}^{i}\partial t_{3}^{i}\partial t_{4}^{j}}\Bigg|_{t_{1}=t_{2}=t_{3}=t_{4}=0},

where
\mu_{\pi}
is simply the sum of all coefficients of all terms of the form
t_{1}^{i}t_{2}^{i}t_{3}^{i}t_{4}^{j}
with
(i,i,i,j)\in\pi
and
i<j
. When all
\boldsymbol{\mu}
are finite plane partitions and
\mu_{4}=\varnothing
, we have
\mu_{\pi}=\sum_{w=(i,i,i,j)\in\pi:i<j}(1-|\{\textrm{legs containing }w\}|)
so (
50
) is a generalization of (
49
).

Remark 6.32
.

For all the verifications of the
\mathrm{DT}
–
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
vertex correspondence in Proposition
6.19
for which
Z_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
is set theoretically supported on
Z(x_{4})
, on the
\mathrm{DT}
side the correct choice of
\pm\sqrt{\cdot}
is given by (
47
) with sign (
50
). We therefore expect that, when
Z_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
is
set theoretically
supported on
Z(x_{4})
, the
\mathrm{DT}
topological vertex with sign choice for which Conjecture
6.17
holds is given by the following formula

\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{DT}}_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}\varnothing}(q)=\sum_{\pi}(-1)^{|\pi|+\mu_{\pi}}[-\widetilde{\mathsf{v}}_{\pi}]\,q^{|\pi|}.

6.5.
Virtual Lefschetz principle – Local

In this section, we study the halved vertex and edge terms
\widetilde{\mathsf{v}}_{\alpha}
,
\widetilde{\mathsf{e}}_{\alpha\beta}
of the previous section for the specialization
y=t_{4}=(t_{1}t_{2}t_{3})^{-1}
. For a 2-dimensional scheme
Z
with torsion filtration
T_{0}(\mathcal{O}_{Z})\subset T_{1}(Z)\subset\mathcal{O}_{Z}
we recall that we denote the corresponding subschemes by
Z\supset Z_{0}\supset Z_{1}
(Section
5.2
).

Proposition 6.33
.

Let
X=\mathbb{C}^{4}
,
Y=Z(x_{4})\cong\mathbb{C}^{3}
,
S=Z(x_{3},x_{4})\cong\mathbb{C}^{2}
. Let
dS\subset Y
be the
d
times thickening of
S
in
Y
.
Let
I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}
be a
T_{X}
-equivariant
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pair on
X
for
q\in\{-1,0\}
with scheme theoretic support
Z
. We assume
Z_{1}=dS
and
Z_{0}\subset Y
. In particular, only the 0-dimensional embedded components of
Z
may not lie scheme-theoretically inside
Y
.

•

Suppose
Z
does
not
lie scheme theoretically in
Y
, which can only happen for
q=-1
. Then
\widetilde{\mathsf{v}}_{I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}|_{y=t_{4}}
has negative
T
-fixed term, in particular

[-\widetilde{\mathsf{v}}_{I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}]\big|_{y=t_{4}}=0.

•

Suppose
Z\subset Y
. Denote by
I_{Y}^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}=[\mathcal{O}_{Y}\to F]
the corresponding
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pair on
Y
. Then there exists a unique 1-dimensional
T_{Y}
-equivariant pair
J^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}=[\mathcal{O}_{Y}\to G]
on
Y
such that

I_{Y}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\cong J^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}(-dS),

where
J^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}
is a 1-dimensional
\mathrm{DT}
pair on
Y
for
q=-1
and a 1-dimensional
\mathrm{PT}
pair on
Y
for
q=0
. Moreover

[-\widetilde{\mathsf{v}}_{I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}]\big|_{y=t_{4}}=[-\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{3D}}_{J^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}],

where
\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{3D}}_{J^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
denotes the equivariant vertex term of 3-dimensional
\mathrm{DT}
theory
[
MNOP
, Sect. 4.9]
resp.
\mathrm{PT}
theory
[
PT2
, Sect. 4.6]
.

Proof.

Let
q=-1
and suppose
Z\subsetneq Y
. Before specializing
y=t_{4}
,
\widetilde{\mathsf{v}}_{I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
has no positive
T
-fixed term (Lemma
6.12
), so we only need to consider terms containing
y
. Using the notation of Definition
6.2
(with
Z:=Z_{\alpha}
,
W:=W_{\alpha}
,
W_{i}:=W_{\alpha\beta_{i}}
,
W_{ij}=Z_{\alpha\beta_{i}\beta_{j}}
), these terms are

-y\overline{W}.

Note that
W
is a Laurent polynomial in
t_{1},t_{2},t_{3},t_{4}
but can have terms with negative coefficients.
However, since only the 0-dimensional embedded components of
Z
may lie outside
Y
, any term in
W
containing
t_{4}
has positive coefficient. Moreover, since
Z\subsetneq Y
, one of these terms is
+t_{4}
. Therefore, after specializing
y=t_{4}
, we conclude that
\widetilde{\mathsf{v}}_{I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
has a negative
T
-fixed term and
[-\widetilde{\mathsf{v}}_{I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}}]|_{y=t_{4}}=0
.

Next, we assume
Z\subset Y
. From Proposition
2.5
, it is clear that there exists a unique 1-dimensional
(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{3}
-equivariant pair
J^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}=[\mathcal{O}_{Y}\to G]
on
Y
such that
I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\cong J^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}(-dS)
(see also the proof of Theorem
4.10
). Continuing the notation of Definition
6.2
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{tr}_{F}

\displaystyle=\frac{1+t_{3}+\cdots+t_{3}^{d-1}}{(1-t_{1})(1-t_{2})}+\frac{W_{1}}{1-t_{1}}+\frac{W_{2}}{1-t_{2}}+W,

\displaystyle\operatorname{tr}_{G}

\displaystyle=\frac{W_{1}t_{3}^{-d}}{1-t_{1}}+\frac{W_{2}t_{3}^{-d}}{1-t_{2}}+Wt_{3}^{-d}.

An explicit calculation shows

\widetilde{\mathsf{v}}_{I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}}-\mathsf{V}_{J^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}}^{\mathrm{3D}}=(t_{4}-y)\overline{W}.

The statement of the proposition follows by setting
y=t_{4}
.
∎

The condition that
Z\subset Y
, except for 0-dimensional embedded components, is necessary as shown by the following example.

Example 6.34
.

Let
Z\subset\mathbb{C}^{4}
be defined by the ideal

I_{Z}=(x_{3},x_{3}x_{4},x_{1}x_{2}x_{4},x_{4}^{2}),

which is a copy of
\mathbb{C}^{2}
with an embedded nodal curve. Then
Z
has an embedded 1-dimensional component which is not contained in
Y=Z(x_{4})
. Moreover,
\widetilde{\mathsf{v}}_{Z}|_{y=t_{4}}
has positive
T
-fixed term
+1
and the specialization
[-\widetilde{\mathsf{v}}_{Z}]|_{y=t_{4}}
is not defined.

As an immediate consequence of Proposition
6.33
, we obtain a dimensional reduction of the
K
-theoretic
\mathrm{DT}
–
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
vertex correspondence of Conjecture
6.17
to the 3-dimensional
K
-theoretic
\mathrm{DT}
–
\mathrm{PT}
(curve) vertex correspondence, which was formulated by Nekrasov-Okounkov
[
NO
, Sect. 2.4.8]
and which generalizes the cohomological version of Pandharipande-Thomas
[
PT2
, Conj. 4]
. The 3-dimensional
K
-theoretic
\mathrm{DT}
–
\mathrm{PT}
vertex correspondence was recently proved in
[
KLT
]
. The 3-dimensional numerical DT/PT vertex correspondence (i.e., for the specialization
t_{1}t_{2}t_{3}=1
) was proved combinatorially by Jenne–Webb–Young
[
JWY
]
. More precisely, we obtain the following.

Corollary 6.35
.

Fix
\mu_{1},\mu_{2}
plane partitions and let
\mu_{3}=\mu_{4}=\varnothing
. Suppose
Z_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
(Definition
5.5
) is scheme theoretically supported in
\mathbb{C}^{3}=Z(x_{4})\subset\mathbb{C}^{4}
. Let
\lambda_{1}
,
\lambda_{2}
be the unique finite partitions with the property that
I_{Z_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}/\mathbb{C}^{3}}=I_{Z_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}/\mathbb{C}^{3}}(-dS)
, for some
d\geq 0
, where
S=Z(x_{3})\subset\mathbb{C}^{3}
and
Z_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\subset\mathbb{C}^{3}
is the Cohen-Macaulay curve determined by
\boldsymbol{\lambda}=(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\varnothing)
.
Then there exist choices of
\pm\sqrt{\cdot}
such that

\frac{\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{DT}}_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\varnothing\varnothing}(q)}{\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{DT}}_{\varnothing\varnothing\varnothing\varnothing}(q)}\Big|_{y=t_{4}}=\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{PT}_{0}}_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\varnothing\varnothing}(q)|_{y=t_{4}}

is equivalent to 2-leg 3-dimensional
K
-theoretic
\mathrm{DT}
–
\mathrm{PT}
vertex correspondence

\frac{\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{DT}}_{\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}\varnothing}(q)}{\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{DT}}_{\varnothing\varnothing\varnothing}(q)}=\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{PT}}_{\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}\varnothing}(q).

The final result of this section concerns a
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
–
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
edge correspondence
for the specialization
y=t_{4}
. Recall that the halved edge term
\widetilde{\mathsf{e}}_{I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
in Definition
6.25
depends on integers
m,m^{\prime},m^{\prime\prime}
, i.e., the weights of the three normal directions (
25
).

Proposition 6.36
.

Let
X=\mathbb{C}^{*}\times\mathbb{C}^{3}
,
Y=Z(x_{4})\cong\mathbb{C}^{*}\times\mathbb{C}^{2}
,
S=Z(x_{3},x_{4})\cong\mathbb{C}^{*}\times\mathbb{C}
. Let
dS\subset Y
be the
d
times thickening of
S
in
Y
.
Let
I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}
be a
T_{X}
-equivariant
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pair on
X
for
q\in\{0,1\}
and denote its scheme theoretic support by
Z
.
32
32

32

Recall that on
X=\mathbb{C}^{*}\times\mathbb{C}^{3}
,
T_{X}
-equivariant
\mathrm{DT}
and
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
pairs are the same.
Assume
Z_{1}=dS
and
m^{\prime\prime}=0
.

•

Suppose
Z
does
not
lie scheme theoretically in
Y
, which can only happen for
q=0
, then
\widetilde{\mathsf{e}}_{I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}|_{y=t_{4}}
has negative
T
-fixed term, in particular

[-\widetilde{\mathsf{e}}_{I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}]\big|_{y=t_{4}}=0.

•

Suppose
Z\subset Y
. Denote by
I_{Y}^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}=[\mathcal{O}_{Y}\to F]
the corresponding
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pair on
Y
with cokernel
Q
. Then there exists a unique 1-dimensional
T_{Y}
-equivariant
\mathrm{DT}=\mathrm{PT}
pair
33
33

33

Note that on
Y=\mathbb{C}^{*}\times\mathbb{C}^{2}
, 1-dimensional
T_{Y}
-equivariant
\mathrm{DT}
and
\mathrm{PT}
pairs coincide.

J^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}=[\mathcal{O}_{Y}\to\mathcal{O}_{C}]
on
Y
such that

\displaystyle I_{Y}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}

\displaystyle\cong I_{C/Y}(-dS),\quad\textrm{for }q=0

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{C}\cdot t_{2}t_{3}

\displaystyle=\overline{Q(dS)},\quad\quad\ \ \ \textrm{for }q=1.

Moreover

[-\widetilde{\mathsf{e}}_{I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}]\big|_{y=t_{4}}=[-\mathsf{E}^{\mathrm{3D}}_{J^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}],

where
\mathsf{E}^{\mathrm{3D}}_{J^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
denotes the equivariant edge term of 3-dimensional
\mathrm{DT}
theory from
[
MNOP
, Sect. 4.9]
or, equivalently
34
34

34

Recall that the equivariant edge terms of 3-dimensional
\mathrm{DT}
and
\mathrm{PT}
theory coincide.
, 3-dimensional
\mathrm{PT}
theory from
[
PT2
, Sect. 4.6]
. In particular, the
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
and
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
edge terms are equal for
y=t_{4}
.

Proof.

Let
q=0
and
Z\subsetneq Y
. We use the notation of Definition
6.2
with
Z_{i}:=Z_{\alpha\beta_{i}}
,
W_{i}:=W_{\alpha\beta_{i}}
,
W_{ij}:=Z_{\alpha\beta_{i}\beta_{j}}
.
Since
\mathsf{e}_{I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
has no positive
T
-fixed terms (Lemma
6.12
), any
T
-fixed term resulting from the specialization
y=t_{4}
comes from

(51)

\frac{t_{1}}{1-t_{1}}\Big(y\overline{W}_{1}\Big)-\frac{1}{1-t_{1}}\Big(y\overline{W}_{1}|_{(t_{2}t_{1}^{-m},t_{3}t_{1}^{-m^{\prime}},t_{4}t_{1}^{-m^{\prime\prime}})}\Big),

where
m^{\prime\prime}=0
. Note that all terms of
W_{1}
are of the form
+t_{2}^{j}t_{3}^{k}t_{4}^{l}
for some
j,k,l\geq 0
(by our conditions on
Z
). Such a term can only produce a
T
-fixed term under the specialization
y=t_{4}
when
j=k=l-1
. Using the fact that
m^{\prime\prime}=0
, the contribution to (
51
) of such a term is

\frac{1}{1-t_{1}}yt_{2}^{-j}t_{3}^{-j}t_{4}^{-j-1}(1-t_{1}^{jm+jm^{\prime}-1})=yt_{2}^{-j}t_{3}^{-j}t_{4}^{-j-1}(-1-t_{1}-\cdots-t_{1}^{-2j}),

where we used that
m^{\prime\prime}=0
and
m+m^{\prime}=-2
by the Calabi-Yau condition. It follows that such a term produces the
T
-fixed term
-1
under the specialization
y=t_{4}
. Since we assume
Z\subsetneq Y
, such terms occur.

Next, we assume
Z\subset Y
. It is easy to see from the the local description of fixed loci (Lemma
5.4
) that there exists a unique 1-dimensional
(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{3}
-equivariant
\mathrm{DT}=\mathrm{PT}
pair
J^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}=[\mathcal{O}_{Y}\to\mathcal{O}_{C}]
on
Y
such that

\displaystyle I_{Y}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}

\displaystyle\cong I_{C/Y}(-dS),\quad\textrm{for }q=0

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{C}\cdot t_{2}t_{3}

\displaystyle=\overline{Q(dS)},\quad\quad\ \ \ \textrm{for }q=1.

Let
C:=\operatorname{tr}_{\mathcal{O}_{C}}
.
Then, for
q=0
, from the definitions of the halved edge term and 3-dimensional edge term, we obtain

\displaystyle\widetilde{e}_{I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}}-\mathsf{E}_{J^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}}^{\mathrm{3D}}=

\displaystyle\frac{t_{1}^{-1}}{1-t_{1}^{-1}}\Big((1-yt_{1}t_{2}t_{3})t_{2}^{-1}t_{3}^{-d-1}\overline{C}\Big)-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-t_{1}^{-1}}\Big(\big((1-yt_{1}t_{2}t_{3})t_{2}^{-1}t_{3}^{-d-1}\overline{C}\big)\Big|_{(t_{1}^{-1},t_{2}t_{1}^{-m},t_{3}t_{1}^{-m^{\prime}})}\Big).

On the other hand, for
q=1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\widetilde{e}_{I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}}-\mathsf{E}_{J^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}}^{\mathrm{3D}}=

\displaystyle\frac{t_{1}^{-1}}{1-t_{1}^{-1}}\Big((1-yt_{1}t_{2}t_{3})t_{3}^{-d}C\Big)-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-t_{1}^{-1}}\Big(\big((1-yt_{1}t_{2}t_{3})t_{3}^{-d}C\big)\Big|_{(t_{1}^{-1},t_{2}t_{1}^{-m},t_{3}t_{1}^{-m^{\prime}})}\Big).

In both cases, we see that
[-\widetilde{e}_{I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}}]|_{y=t_{4}}
is well-defined and equal to
[-\mathsf{E}_{J^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}}^{\mathrm{3D}}]
.
∎

The specialization
y=t_{4}
provides a dimensional reduction, which we think of as a
local
virtual Lefschetz principle as opposed to the global virtual Lefschetz principles studied in Sections
4.1
and
4.2
.

6.6.
Local projective plane

Consider
X=\mathrm{Tot}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(-2)\oplus\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(-1))
. Note that

v=(0,0,\gamma,\beta,n-\gamma\cdot\mathrm{td}_{2}(X))\in H_{c}^{*}(X,\mathbb{Q})

has the property that
\gamma=d[\mathbb{P}^{2}]
and
\beta=m[\mathbb{P}^{1}]
. We therefore write
\gamma=d
and
\beta=m
. Then, for
q\in\{-1,0,1\}
, the virtual dimension of
{\curly P}^{(q)}_{v}(X)
equals

\mathrm{vd}=n-d^{2}.

For
q=1
, and fixed
d,m
, there are only finitely many
n
such that
\mathrm{vd}\geq 0

[
BKP
, Prop. 2.14]
.

For a fixed
d
, we are interested in the generating series

\displaystyle\mathsf{G}^{\mathrm{PT}_{q}}_{d}(q,Q,y):=\sum_{m,n}\langle\!\langle\mathcal{O}_{X}\rangle\!\rangle_{d,m,n}^{\mathrm{PT}_{q}}Q^{m}q^{n},

for
q\in\{0,1\}
. By Proposition
3.12
, the moduli spaces
{\curly P}^{(q)}_{v}(X)
are projective. Hence the specialization
t_{1}=t_{2}=t_{3}=t_{4}=1
is well-defined and we will always make this specialization.

We use the description of fixed loci (Section
5
) and the vertex formalism (Section
6.1
) to calculate part of these generating series. We focus mostly on the calculation of
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
invariants. Since
X
is the canonical bundle over a smooth quasi-projective toric 3-fold, we can in fact use Section
6.4
, i.e., Proposition
6.30
.

Remark 6.37
.

Since
X
is a local surface, Theorem
5.29
(3) implies that, for
q=0
,
{\curly P}^{(q)}_{v}(X)^{T_{X}}={\curly P}^{(q)}_{v}(X)^{T}
is 0-dimensional and reduced. For
q=1
, the calculations in this section depend on Conjecture
5.30
.

We used an implementation into a Maple program to show that there are signs such that the following formula holds:

\displaystyle[y]^{-1}\mathsf{G}^{\mathrm{PT}_{0}}_{1}=

\displaystyle\,qQ^{\frac{3}{2}}+\Big(q^{3}+(y^{\frac{3}{2}}+y^{-\frac{3}{2}})q^{4}+(y^{2}+y^{-2})q^{5}+(y^{\frac{5}{2}}+y^{-\frac{5}{2}})q^{6}+\cdots\Big)Q^{\frac{5}{2}}+

\displaystyle\Big(0\cdot q^{4}+0\cdot q^{5}+\cdots\Big)Q^{\frac{7}{2}}+\cdots\,,

where
\cdots
means higher order terms in
q
or
Q
. The term with
Q^{\frac{3}{2}}
has no embedded curve, i.e.,
\mathrm{PT}_{0}=\mathrm{PT}_{1}
. The terms with
Q^{\frac{5}{2}}
have one embedded
\mathbb{P}^{1}
. The terms with
Q^{\frac{7}{2}}
have two embedded
\mathbb{P}^{1}
s. Based on this, it is reasonable to expect that for
m=\frac{5}{2}
and any
n>3
, we have

(52)

[y]^{-1}\langle\!\langle\mathcal{O}_{X}\rangle\!\rangle_{1,\frac{5}{2},n}^{\mathrm{PT}_{0}}=y^{\frac{n-1}{2}}+y^{-\frac{n-1}{2}}.

Equation (
52
) will be proved using different methods a sequel to this paper. We note that the two zeroes at the end are non-trivial. E.g., the term
0\cdot q^{5}Q^{\frac{7}{2}}
involves the summation of 15 fixed points which, individually, have non-zero contributions.

Next, we used the vertex formalism to show that there are signs such that:

(53)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathsf{G}^{\mathrm{PT}_{1}}_{1}=&[y]qQ^{\frac{3}{2}}+\Big(2[y]q+[y^{2}]q^{2}+[y^{3}]q^{3}\Big)Q^{\frac{5}{2}}+\\
&\Big(...q+...q^{2}+...q^{3}+2[y^{4}]q^{4}+[y^{5}]q^{5}+[y^{6}]q^{6}\Big)Q^{\frac{7}{2}}+\cdots,\end{split}

where
...q^{i}
means that we did not determine the coefficient. In a sequel to this paper, we determine
\mathsf{G}^{\mathrm{PT}_{1}}_{1}
up to all orders (essentially by using the nested description of Proposition
2.7
). We present the answer. Let
S
be a smooth projective surface and
\mathcal{Z}\subset S\times\operatorname{Hilb}^{m}(S)
the universal subscheme. Denote the corresponding ideal sheaf by
I_{\mathcal{Z}}
and let
\pi:S\times\operatorname{Hilb}^{m}(S)\to\operatorname{Hilb}^{m}(S)
be the projection. For any
L\in\mathrm{Pic}(S)
, consider the complex

T(L):=R\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{\pi}(I_{\mathcal{Z}},I_{\mathcal{Z}}\otimes L)_{0}[1],

which is in fact a vector bundle concentrated in degree 0.
Then Carlsson-Okounkov
[
CO
]
showed

\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\int_{\operatorname{Hilb}^{m}(S)}c_{2m}(T(L))q^{m}=\Bigg(\prod_{m=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{1-q^{m}}\Bigg)^{e(S)+L(L-K_{S})},

where
e(S)
is the topological Euler characteristic of
S
. Denote the coefficient of
q^{m}
on the left hand side by
\mathsf{CO}(S,L)_{m}
. Then in a sequel we show that

\displaystyle\langle\!\langle\mathcal{O}_{X}\rangle\!\rangle_{1,\frac{3}{2}+\delta,n}^{\mathrm{PT}_{1}}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{CO}_{\chi(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(\delta))-n}(\mathbb{P}^{2},\mathcal{O}(-1))\cdot[y^{n}]

\displaystyle=\mathsf{CO}_{\chi(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(\delta))-n}(\mathbb{P}^{2},\mathcal{O}(-1))[y](y^{\frac{n-1}{2}}+\cdots+y^{-\frac{n-1}{2}}),

for all
\delta\geq 0
and
1\leq n\leq\chi(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(\delta))
. This formula is consistent with the toric calculations.

The proofs of (
52
) and (
53
) in the sequels strongly depend on the fact that, so far, we considered the
irreducible
class
\gamma=[\mathbb{P}^{2}]
. The main strength of the vertex formalism is that it allows us to consider
\gamma=d[\mathbb{P}^{2}]
with
d>1
.

For
d=2
, we have shown that

\displaystyle[y]^{-4}\mathsf{G}^{\mathrm{PT}_{0}}_{2}=q^{4}Q^{4}+\Big(2(y^{\frac{1}{2}}+y^{-\frac{1}{2}})^{3}q^{7}+\cdots\Big)Q^{5}+\cdots.

The term
q^{7}Q^{5}
involves contributions of the zero section
\mathbb{P}^{2}\subset X
thickened in the direction of
\mathcal{O}(-1)
as well as in the direction of
\mathcal{O}(-2)
. In the former case, it involves contributions with an embedded curve thickened in the direction of
\mathcal{O}(-1)
as well as in the direction of
\mathcal{O}(-2)
. Moreover, the signs in Proposition
6.30
for fixed points scheme theoretically inside the zero section
\mathrm{Tot}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(-1))\subset X
and those which are not are
opposite
. Next, we determined the following terms of the
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
generating series for
d=2

\displaystyle[y]^{-4}\mathsf{G}^{\mathrm{PT}_{1}}_{2}=

\displaystyle\,q^{4}Q^{4}+\Big(4q^{4}+2(y^{\frac{1}{2}}+y^{-\frac{1}{2}})q^{5}+2(y+y^{-1})q^{6}+

\displaystyle 2(y^{\frac{1}{2}}+y^{-\frac{1}{2}})(2y+1+2y^{-1})q^{7}\Big)Q^{5}+

\displaystyle\Big(...q^{4}+\cdots+2(y^{\frac{1}{2}}+y^{-\frac{1}{2}})(y+y^{-1})(5y+1+5y^{-1})q^{9}+

\displaystyle(7y^{3}+12y^{2}+15y+16+15y^{-1}+12y^{-2}+7y^{-3})q^{10}+

\displaystyle 2(y^{\frac{1}{2}}+y^{-\frac{1}{2}})(4y^{3}+3y^{2}+6y+4+6y^{-1}+3y^{-2}+4y^{-3})q^{11}\Big)Q^{6}+\cdots.

The calculation involves thickenings of the zero section
\mathbb{P}^{2}
in the
\mathcal{O}(-1)
as well as the
\mathcal{O}(-2)
direction. The computation of the coefficient of
q^{9}Q^{6}
involves 48 fixed points. For
d=3
we found

\displaystyle[y]^{-9}\mathsf{G}^{\mathrm{PT}_{1}}_{3}=

\displaystyle\,\Big(0q^{9}+3(y^{\frac{1}{2}}+y^{-\frac{1}{2}})q^{10}\Big)Q^{\frac{15}{2}}+\Big(2q^{9}+18(y^{\frac{1}{2}}+y^{-\frac{1}{2}})q^{10}+

\displaystyle 9(y^{\frac{1}{2}}+y^{-\frac{1}{2}})^{2}q^{11}+9(y^{\frac{1}{2}}+y^{-\frac{1}{2}})(y+y^{-1})q^{12}+

\displaystyle 3(3y^{2}+2y-3+2y^{-1}+3y^{-2})q^{13}+

\displaystyle 3(y^{\frac{1}{2}}+y^{-\frac{1}{2}})(7y^{2}+8y+12+8y^{-1}+7y^{-2})q^{14}\Big)Q^{\frac{17}{2}}+\cdots.

The calculation of the coefficient of
q^{9}Q^{\frac{17}{2}}
involves 51 fixed points. For
d=4
, we obtained:

\displaystyle[y]^{-16}\mathsf{G}^{\mathrm{PT}_{1}}_{4}=

\displaystyle\,q^{16}Q^{11}+\Big(8q^{16}+4(y^{\frac{1}{2}}+y^{-\frac{1}{2}})q^{17}+4(y+y^{-1})q^{18}+

\displaystyle 4(y^{\frac{3}{2}}+y^{-\frac{3}{2}})q^{19}+10(y^{2}+8y+12+8y^{-1}+y^{-2})q^{20}\Big)Q^{12}+\cdots.

Generally, the poles in the equivariant parameters
t_{i}
in the calculations only cancel when the contributions of the fixed points are summed with very specific signs, which we expect to be induced from global canonical orientations. We observe that all terms calculated in this section are invariant under
y\to 1/y
and, more generally, are consistent with the structure described in Proposition
3.6
. This is evidence for the expectation that the sign choices found in this section are induced from global orientations.

We note that all coefficients appearing in the calculations of this section are
integer
. This is no longer true when one does not include the
K
-theoretic tautological insertion (Definition
3.3
). E.g. for
d=1
,
m=\frac{7}{2}
,
n=5
, we have
\chi(\curly P_{v}^{(1)}(X),\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{vir}})=\frac{3}{128}
(for the correct sign choice).
